\Delta,D\geq 0
and
D
is big. Then there exists an eventually birational graded linear series of
D
such that
(X,\Delta+\Gamma)
is log canonical for all
m\in M(V_{\bullet})
and all
\Gamma\in\frac{1}{m}|V_{m}|
.

Proof.

Since
D
is big, we may write
D=A+B
where
A
is an ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor and
B\geq 0
. Let
\overline{X}
be a normal projective compactification of
X
such that
A
extends to an ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\overline{A}
(for example, consider the morphism from
X
into some projective space induced by the linear system
|qA|
for some sufficiently positive and divisible integer
q
, and take
\overline{X}
to be the normalization of the closure of the image). Then
D
also extends to a big
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\overline{D}=\overline{A}+\overline{B}
on
\overline{X}
, where
\overline{B}
is the closure of
B
. Let
V_{m}\subseteq H^{0}(X,mD)
be the image of the restriction map
H^{0}(\overline{X},m\overline{D})\to H^{0}(X,mD)
. Since
(X,\Delta+D)
is klt, by Lemma
2.7
applied to some log resolution of the pair
(X,\Delta+D)
, we know that there exists some sufficiently large integer
\ell>0
such that
(X,\Delta+\left(1-\frac{1}{\ell}\right)D+\frac{1}{\ell}G)
is log canonical for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
and all
G\in\frac{1}{m}|V_{m}|
. Define a new graded linear series
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
of
D
by

|V^{\prime}_{m}|=m\left(1-\frac{1}{\ell}\right)D+|V_{m/\ell}|

when
\ell
divides
m
, and
V^{\prime}_{m}=0
otherwise. Since
\overline{D}
is big, both
V_{\bullet}
and
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
are eventually birational. By construction,
(X,\Delta+\Gamma)
is log canonical for all
m\in\ell\cdot\mathbb{N}^{+}
and
\Gamma\in\frac{1}{m}|V^{\prime}_{m}|
. Thus
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
satisfies the desired conditions.
∎

We are now ready to prove the theorem.

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

We first prove (3). Since
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier, so is
D^{\prime}
. By Lemma
3.5
, we can find an eventually birational graded linear series
V_{\bullet}
of
D
such that
(X,\Delta+\Gamma)
is log canonical for all
m\in M(V_{\bullet})
and all
\Gamma\in\frac{1}{m}|V_{m}|
. Since
\Gamma\sim_{\mathbb{R}}D
and
\varphi
is an MMP type contraction between
(X,\Delta+D)
and
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+D^{\prime})
, by Lemma
2.13
it is also an MMP type contraction between
(X,\Delta+\Gamma)
and
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\Gamma^{\prime})
where
\Gamma^{\prime}:=\varphi_{*}\Gamma
. It follows that
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\Gamma^{\prime})
is log canonical and thus
\alpha(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime};\varphi_{*}V_{\bullet})\geq 1
. Note that
\varphi_{*}V_{\bullet}
is a graded linear series of
D^{\prime}
. By Proposition
3.3
, we obtain

\widehat{\rm vol}(x^{\prime},X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\geq\mathrm{vol}(\varphi_{*}V_{\bullet})=\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})

for all closed points
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}
. Hence (3) holds with
\varepsilon:=\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})>0
.

Next we prove (1). Let
n=\dim X
. Take a small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modification
\pi\colon X^{\prime\prime}\to X^{\prime}
(which exists by
[
8
]
*Corollary 1.4.3) and let
\Delta^{\prime\prime},D^{\prime\prime}
be the strict transforms of
\Delta^{\prime},D^{\prime}
respectively. Then
K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier and the induced map
(X,\Delta+D)\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+D^{\prime\prime})
is also an MMP type contraction. By the base point free theorem
[
42
]
*Theorem 3.24, the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor
L^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime}
is the same as the Cartier index of
\pi^{*}L^{\prime}
on
X^{\prime\prime}
. Now for any closed point
x^{\prime\prime}\in X^{\prime\prime}
we have
\widehat{\rm vol}(x^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\geq\varepsilon
by (3) and hence the Cartier index of
\pi^{*}L^{\prime}
is at most
\left\lfloor\frac{n^{n}}{\varepsilon}\right\rfloor
at
x^{\prime\prime}
by Theorem
2.4
. Thus (1) holds with
r=\left\lfloor\frac{n^{n}}{\varepsilon}\right\rfloor!
.

Finally, by Theorem
2.5
, there exists some constant
A>0
(depending only on
n,\varepsilon
) such that
\mathrm{mld}(x^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})\leq A
for all points
x^{\prime\prime}\in X^{\prime\prime}
. As
\pi
is small, we have
\pi^{*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}+D^{\prime})=K_{X^{\prime\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime\prime}+D^{\prime\prime}
, hence

\mathrm{mld}(x^{\prime},X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+D^{\prime})\leq\mathrm{mld}(x^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime}+D^{\prime\prime})\leq\mathrm{mld}(x^{\prime\prime},X^{\prime\prime},\Delta^{\prime\prime})

for any
x^{\prime\prime}\in\pi^{-1}(x)
. Thus it also holds that
\mathrm{mld}(x^{\prime},X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+D^{\prime})\leq A
, which gives (2).
∎

Remark 3.6
.

It is not clear if the local volume satisfies any monotonicity in the MMP; at least the naïve versions fail. For example, there exist flops
X_{1}\dashrightarrow X_{2}
and valuations
v
whose center is a closed point on both
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
but
\widehat{\rm vol}_{X_{1}}(v)\neq\widehat{\rm vol}_{X_{2}}(v)
. To get an explicit example, let
Z
be the cone over the smooth quadric surface
Q\cong\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{3}
and consider the Atiyah flop
X_{1}\dashrightarrow X_{2}
between its two small resolutions. Then
Z
is a toric variety and most toric valuations
v
satisfy
\widehat{\rm vol}_{X_{1}}(v)\neq\widehat{\rm vol}_{X_{2}}(v)
. By adding appropriate boundaries to
X_{i}
we can turn the flop
X_{1}\dashrightarrow X_{2}
into either a flip or an anti-flip. Thus there exist flips
(X,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(X^{+},\Delta^{+})
where the normalized volume of valuations increases (resp. decreases).

Alternatively, one may ask whether the smallest local volume
\inf_{x\in X}\widehat{\rm vol}(x,X,\Delta)
of the klt pair
(X,\Delta)
is monotone in the MMP. The answer is again negative. Indeed, by
[
52
]
*Theorem A.4, we have
\inf_{x\in X}\widehat{\rm vol}(x,X,\Delta)\leq n^{n}
with equality if and only if
X
is smooth and
\Delta=0
. There are flips
X\dashrightarrow X^{+}
such that
X
is singular (resp. smooth) whereas
X^{+}
is smooth (resp. singular), in which case the smallest local volume increases (resp. decreases) after the flips. For some explicit examples, consider the total space
\widetilde{X}
of the line bundle
L=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{m}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}}(-1,-2)
on
\mathbb{P}^{m}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}
. Let
X_{1}
(resp.
X_{2}
) be obtained from
\widetilde{X}
by contracting the zero section
E\cong\mathbb{P}^{m}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}
using the first (resp. second) projection of
\mathbb{P}^{m}\times\mathbb{P}^{n}
. Then
X_{1}
is smooth and
X_{2}
is singular. On the other hand, a direct calculation shows that the induced birational map
X_{1}\dashrightarrow X_{2}
(resp.
X_{2}\dashrightarrow X_{1}
) is a flip if and only if
2m+1<n
(resp.
2m+1>n
).

3.2.
Log canonical volume

Although the local volume does not seem to satisfy monotonicity in the MMP, we can nonetheless introduce a global variant that does. The definition is motivated by the argument in the previous subsection. We begin with an auxiliary definition.

Definition 3.7
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a pair and let
L
be a big
\mathbb{R}
-divisor such that
K_{X}+\Delta+L
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier. A graded linear series
V_{\bullet}
of
L
is called
admissible
(with respect to
(X,\Delta)
) if it is eventually birational and
(X,\Delta+\Gamma)
is log canonical for all
m\in M(V_{\bullet})
and all
\Gamma\in\frac{1}{m}|V_{m}|
. Note that the latter condition is equivalent to
\alpha(X,\Delta;V_{\bullet})\geq 1
when
K_{X}+\Delta
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
and
L
be as in Definition
3.7
. Then an admissible graded linear series of
L
exists if and only if there exists some
0\leq D\sim_{\mathbb{R}}L
such that
(X,\Delta+D)
is klt.

Proof.

If there exists some
0\leq D\sim_{\mathbb{R}}L
such that
(X,\Delta+D)
is klt, then we can choose such
D
with
D\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}L
. Thus one direction is implied by Lemma
3.5
. For the other direction, let
V_{\bullet}
be an admissible graded linear series of
L
. By assumption, there exists some
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that the rational map
f\colon X\dashrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}
induced by the linear system
|V_{m}|
is birational onto its image. Let
\pi\colon X^{\prime}\to X
be a log resolution of
(X,\Delta)
that resolve the indeterminacy of
f
and let
g\colon X^{\prime}\to\mathbb{P}^{N}
the induced morphism. For any prime divisor
F
on
X^{\prime}
, let

a_{F}:=-\sup\left\{a(F,X,\Delta+D)\middle|\ D\in\frac{1}{m}|V_{m}|\right\},

and let
\Delta^{\prime}:=\sum a_{F}F
. Note that this is a finite sum since
a_{F}=0
unless
F
is
\pi
-exceptional or the strict transform of some component of
\Delta+D
for any
D\in|V_{m}|
. We also note that
\pi_{*}\Delta^{\prime}-\Delta
is the fixed part of
|V_{m}|
.

Let
L^{\prime}:=\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+L)-(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime})
. Then the linear series
V_{m}
of
mL
induces a linear series
V^{\prime}_{m}
of
mL^{\prime}
defined by

|V^{\prime}_{m}|=\{\pi^{*}(mK_{X}+m\Delta+D)-mK_{X^{\prime}}-m\Delta^{\prime}\,|\,D\in|V_{m}|\},

and the morphism
g
is induced by
|V^{\prime}_{m}|
. In particular, the linear system
|V^{\prime}_{m}|
is base point free. We claim that the sub-pair
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is klt. In fact, since
V_{\bullet}
is admissible, we know that
(X,\Delta+D)
is log canonical for any positive integer
\ell
and all
D\in\frac{1}{\ell m}|V_{\ell m}|
. This implies that for any hypersurface
H\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{N}
of degree
\ell
whose support does not contain
g(X^{\prime})
, the sub-pair
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\frac{1}{\ell m}g^{*}H)
is log canonical, as
\frac{1}{\ell m}g^{*}H\sim L^{\prime}
and thus the sub-pair is the crepant pullback of
(X,\Delta+D)
where
D=\pi_{*}\Delta^{\prime}-\Delta+\frac{1}{\ell m}\pi_{*}g^{*}H\in\frac{1}{\ell m}|V_{\ell m}|
. If
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is not klt, then there exists some prime divisor
E
over
X^{\prime}
such that
A_{X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}}(E)\leq 0
. As
g
is a birational morphism, we can choose some hypersurface
H
as above of sufficiently large degree such that
g^{*}H
contains the center of
E
. But then
A_{X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\frac{1}{\ell m}g^{*}H}(E)<0
and hence
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\frac{1}{\ell m}g^{*}H)
cannot be log canonical, a contradiction. This proves the lemma.
∎

We now introduce the global invariant that behaves well in the MMP.

Definition 3.9
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a pair and let
L
be a big
\mathbb{R}
-divisor such that
K_{X}+\Delta+L
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier. We define the
log canonical volume
of
L
with respect to
(X,\Delta)
, denoted by
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L)
, as follows:

(1)

If
(X,\Delta+D)
is klt for some
0\leq D\sim_{\mathbb{R}}L
, we set

\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L):=\sup\{\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})\,|\,V_{\bullet}\mbox{ is an admissible graded linear series of }L\}.

In this case
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L)>0
by Lemma
3.5
.

(2)

Otherwise, by Lemma
3.8
, there are no admissible graded linear series of
L
and we set
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L):=0
.

While we do not need the next statement in this paper, we note that the log canonical volume defined above only depends on the numerical equivalence class of
L
. Throughout the rest of this subsection, let
n=\dim X
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
and
L
be as in Definition
3.9
, and let
L^{\prime}
be another
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
L^{\prime}-L
extends to an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor in
\mathrm{Pic}^{0}(\overline{X})\otimes\mathbb{R}
for some projective compactification
\overline{X}
of
X
. Then

\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L)=\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L^{\prime}).

Proof.

It suffices to show that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L^{\prime})\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L)
, as by symmetry we also get the reverse inequality. To prove this, we may assume that there exists some big
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
0\leq D\sim_{\mathbb{R}}L
such that
(X,\Delta+D)
is klt, and it is enough to show that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L^{\prime})\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{\ell}\right)^{n}\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})
for any sufficiently large integer
\ell
and any admissible graded linear series
V_{\bullet}
of
L
. Since
L^{\prime}-L\in\mathrm{Pic}^{0}(\overline{X})\otimes\mathbb{R}
, we can find some
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
0\leq D^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}\ell(L^{\prime}-L)+L
such that
(X,\Delta+D^{\prime})
is also klt. To see this, write
D=A+B
where
A
is ample and
B\geq 0
such that the pair
(X,\Delta+B)
is also klt, then
A+\ell(L^{\prime}-L)
is still ample and we can take
D^{\prime}=A^{\prime}+B
for some general
0\leq A^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}A+\ell(L^{\prime}-L)
. By construction,
(1-\frac{1}{\ell})L+\frac{1}{\ell}D^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}L^{\prime}
. For any sufficiently divisible integer
m
, let
V^{\prime}_{m}
be the linear series of
mL^{\prime}
defined by
|V^{\prime}_{m}|=|V_{(1-\frac{1}{\ell})m}|+\frac{m}{\ell}D^{\prime}
. Then
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
is admissible by the convexity of log canonicity. It follows that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L^{\prime})\geq\mathrm{vol}(V^{\prime}_{\bullet})=\left(1-\frac{1}{\ell}\right)^{n}\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})
as desired.
∎

The proof of Theorem
3.2
can be reformulated in terms of some properties of the log canonical volume
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L)
. These include its monotonicity in the MMP, and its comparison with the local volume.

Lemma 3.11
.

Let
\varphi\colon(X,\Delta+D)\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+D^{\prime})
be an MMP type contraction between klt pairs. Assume that
\Delta,\Delta^{\prime}\geq 0
,
D
is big, and
D^{\prime}=\varphi_{*}D
. Then
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}}(D^{\prime})\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(D)
.

Proof.

If
V_{\bullet}
is an admissible graded linear series of
D
, then
\varphi_{*}V_{\bullet}
is an admissible graded linear series of
D^{\prime}
as in the proof of Theorem
3.2
.
∎

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair and let
D\geq 0
be a big
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
. Then for any closed point
x\in X
we have
\widehat{\rm vol}(x,X,\Delta)\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(D)
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from Proposition
3.3
.
∎

We make a few more elementary observations on the log canonical volume
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(L)
that will be useful in the next several sections.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair such that
\Delta
is big. Then the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor on
X
is at most
\left\lfloor\frac{n^{n}}{\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)}\right\rfloor!
.

Proof.

Let
X^{\prime}\to X
be a small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modification and let
\Delta^{\prime}
be the strict transform of
\Delta
. By Lemma
3.12
, we have
\widehat{\rm vol}(x^{\prime},X^{\prime})\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})
for any closed point
x^{\prime}\in X^{\prime}
, hence by Theorem
2.4
we know that the Cartier index of any Weil divisor on
X^{\prime}
is at most
\left\lfloor\frac{n^{n}}{\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})}\right\rfloor!
. Note that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})=\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)
by Lemma
3.11
. The result then follows from the base point free theorem as in the proof of Theorem
3.2
.
∎

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair such that
\Delta
is big, let
\lambda\in[0,1)
, and let
0\leq\Delta_{0}\leq\lambda\Delta
be an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor. Then

\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta_{0}}(\Delta-\Delta_{0})\geq(1-\lambda)^{n}\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta).

Proof.

If
V_{\bullet}
is an admissible graded linear series of
\Delta-\Delta_{0}
with respect to
(X,\Delta_{0})
, then its image
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
under the natural inclusion
H^{0}(X,m(\Delta-\Delta_{0}))\hookrightarrow H^{0}(X,m\Delta)
is an admissible graded linear series of
\Delta
with respect to
X
. This gives the first inequality. To prove the second inequality we may assume that
\lambda\in\mathbb{Q}
. For any admissible graded linear series
V_{\bullet}
of
\Delta
, let
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
be the graded linear series of
\Delta-\Delta_{0}
defined by
|V^{\prime}_{m}|=|V_{(1-\lambda)m}|+m(\lambda\Delta-\Delta_{0})
for sufficiently divisible
m\in\mathbb{N}
. By convexity of log canonicity, we know that
(X,\lambda\Delta+(1-\lambda)D)
is log canonical for all
m\in M(V_{\bullet})
and
D\in\frac{1}{m}|V_{m}|
. This implies that
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
is admissible with respect to
(X,\Delta_{0})
. Since
V_{\bullet}
is arbitrary and
\mathrm{vol}(V^{\prime}_{\bullet})=(1-\lambda)^{n}\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})
, the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
(X,\Delta+D)
be a klt pair where
\Delta\geq 0
and
D
is big. Let
H
be an ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
. Then

\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(D+H)\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(D).

Proof.

Let
\ell
be a sufficiently large and divisible integer. It is enough to show that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(D+H)\geq(1-\frac{1}{\ell})^{n}\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})
for any admissible graded linear series
V_{\bullet}
of
D
. Since
(X,\Delta+D)
is klt,
H
is ample, and
\ell
is sufficiently large and divisible, by Bertini’s theorem we can find some
H_{0}\in|\ell H|
such that
(X,\Delta+D+H_{0})
is log canonical. Define a graded linear series
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
of
D+H
by
|V^{\prime}_{m}|=|V_{\left(1-\frac{1}{\ell}\right)m}|+\frac{m}{\ell}(D+H_{0})
for all
m\in\ell\cdot\mathbb{N}
. Then
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
is admissible by convexity of log canonicity as before and this proves the result.
∎

We also prove a semi-continuity result for the log canonical volume, which allows us to reduce many questions to the case of rational coefficients. Let
\mathrm{Div}(X)
be the free group generated by the prime divisors on
X
.

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a pair, let
\Lambda\subseteq\mathrm{Div}(X)_{\mathbb{R}}
be a finite dimensional subspace, and let
\mathcal{L}(\Lambda)\subseteq\Lambda
be the subset of
\mathbb{R}
-divisors
D\geq 0
such that
(X,\Delta+D)
is klt and
D
is big. Then the function
D\mapsto\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(D)
is lower semi-continuous in
\mathcal{L}(\Lambda)
.

Proof.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
be a sufficiently large integer. It suffices to show that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(D^{\prime})\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{\ell}\right)^{n}\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(D)
whenever
D^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}(\Lambda)
is sufficiently close to
D
. To see this, we may assume that
D^{\prime}\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{\ell}\right)D
and

\left(X,\Delta+\ell\left(D^{\prime}-\left(1-\frac{1}{\ell}\right)D\right)\right)=(X,\Delta+D+\ell(D^{\prime}-D))

is klt. If
V_{\bullet}
is an admissible graded linear series of
D
, and define

\left|V^{\prime}_{m}\right|=\left|V_{\left(1-\frac{1}{\ell}\right)m}\right|+mD^{\prime}-m\left(1-\frac{1}{\ell}\right)D

when
\ell
divides
m
(otherwise set
V^{\prime}_{m}=0
), then by convexity of log canonicity as above we see that
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
is an admissible graded linear series of
D^{\prime}
and by construction
\mathrm{vol}(V^{\prime}_{\bullet})\geq(1-\frac{1}{\ell})^{n}\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})
. This proves the result.
∎

3.3.
Explicit bounds

In the remaining part of this section, we prove some effective versions of Theorem
3.2
, giving explicit bounds on the invariants at least when the pair is log smooth and projective (in general, one can reduce to this case by passing to a log resolution of some projective compactification). We also prove an explicit index bound for
3
-fold MMP. These will be useful in Section
6
where we bound the number of steps in certain MMP.

We start with an explicit bound of the log canonical volume.

Lemma 3.17
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a projective log smooth klt pair of dimension
n
such that
\Delta
is big, and let
H
be a very ample divisor on
X
. Then

\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)\geq\left\lceil\frac{(\Delta\cdot H^{n-1})}{1-\max\mathrm{Coeff}(\Delta)}\right\rceil^{-n}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(\Delta)

Proof.

Since
H
is very ample, we have
\mathrm{mult}_{x}D\leq(\Delta\cdot H^{n-1})
for any
x\in X
and any
0\leq D\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta
. By Lemma
2.7
, this gives

\mathrm{lct}(X,\Delta;D)\geq\frac{1-\max\mathrm{Coeff}(\Delta)}{(\Delta\cdot H^{n-1})}.

Thus in the proof of Lemma
3.5
we can take
\ell
to be
\left\lceil\frac{(\Delta\cdot H^{n-1})}{1-\max\mathrm{Coeff}(\Delta)}\right\rceil
. It then follows from the argument in
loc. cit.
that there exists an admissible linear series
V_{\bullet}
of
\Delta
with
\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})\geq\ell^{-n}\mathrm{vol}(\Delta)
, hence
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)\geq\ell^{-n}\mathrm{vol}(\Delta)
as desired.
∎

Corollary 3.18
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a projective log smooth klt pair of dimension
n
, and let
N
be a positive integer. Assume that
N\Delta
has integer coefficients,
\mathrm{vol}(\Delta)\geq\frac{1}{N}
, and
(\Delta\cdot H^{n-1})\leq N
for some very ample divisor
H
on
X
. Then in any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP, the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor is at most
(n^{n}N^{2n+1})!
.

Proof.

By assumption, we have
1-\max\mathrm{Coeff}(\Delta)\geq\frac{1}{N}
. Hence by Lemma
3.17
and our assumptions, we obtain
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)\geq N^{-2n-1}
. The result then follows from Lemmas
3.11
and
3.13
.
∎

By a similar argument, we can also bound the index explicitly in a general type MMP.

Lemma 3.19
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a projective klt pair of dimension
n
such that
K_{X}+\Delta
is big. Then for any sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP
\varphi\colon(X,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(Y,\Delta_{Y})
and any closed point
y\in Y
, we have

\widehat{\rm vol}(y,Y,\Delta_{Y})\geq\alpha(X,\Delta;K_{X}+\Delta)^{n}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(K_{X}+\Delta).

Proof.

Let
\alpha:=\alpha(X,\Delta;K_{X}+\Delta)
and let
V_{\bullet}
be the complete linear series of
K_{X}+\Delta
, i.e.
V_{m}=H^{0}(X,m(K_{X}+\Delta))
. Then for any positive integer
m
and any
D\in\frac{1}{m}|V_{m}|
, the pair
(X,\Delta+\alpha D)
is log canonical, and the
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP is also a
(K_{X}+\Delta+\alpha D)
-MMP. It follows that
(Y,\Delta_{Y}+\alpha D_{Y})
is also log canonical, hence
\alpha(Y,\Delta;\varphi_{*}V_{\bullet})\geq\alpha
. Note that
\mathrm{vol}(\varphi_{*}V_{\bullet})=\mathrm{vol}(K_{X}+\Delta)
. The result then follows from Proposition
3.3
.
∎

Corollary 3.20
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a projective log smooth klt pair of dimension
n
, and let
N
be a positive integer. Assume that
N\Delta
has integer coefficients,
\mathrm{vol}(K_{X}+\Delta)\geq\frac{1}{N}
, and
((K_{X}+\Delta)\cdot H^{n-1})\leq N
for some very ample divisor
H
on
X
. Then in any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP, the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor is at most
(n^{n}N^{2n+1})!
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.7
, we have

\alpha(X,\Delta;K_{X}+\Delta)\geq\frac{1-\max\mathrm{Coeff}(\Delta)}{((K_{X}+\Delta)\cdot H^{n-1})}\geq\frac{1}{N^{2}}

as in the proof of Lemma
3.17
. We then conclude by Lemma
3.19
and Theorem
2.4
.
∎

Finally, we show that the Cartier index is uniformly bounded in
3
-dimensional MMP (without any general type or bigness assumption). For any klt pair
(X,\Delta)
, we set

(3.1)

e(X,\Delta):=\#\left\{\mbox{Exceptional prime divisors $E$ over $X$ with $a(E,X,\Delta)\leq 0$}\right\}.

We also define
e_{+}(X,\Delta)
as the sum of
e(X,\Delta)
and the number of irreducible components of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
. By
[
42
, Proposition 2.36]
,
e(X,\Delta)<+\infty
.

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair of dimension
3
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varepsilon>0
. Assume that there are at most
m
divisors
E
over
X
such that
a(E,X,\Delta)\in(0,\varepsilon)
. Then the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor on
X
is at most

\left(\left\lceil\frac{m+2}{\varepsilon}\right\rceil!\right)^{2^{e(X,\Delta)}}\left(\frac{3}{\mathrm{mld}(X,\Delta)}\right)^{2^{e(X,\Delta)}-1}.

Proof.

This follows from the same proof of
[
43
]
*Theorem (6.11.5), replacing the base case (i.e. when
e(X,\Delta)=0
and hence
(X,\Delta)
is terminal)
[
43
]
*Lemma 6.9.7 of the induction by
[
56
]
*Corollary 3.4.
∎

Corollary 3.22
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair of dimension
3
. Let
\varepsilon
be the smallest positive discrepancy of
(X,\Delta)
(i.e. for any prime divisor
E
over
X
we have
a(E,X,\Delta)>0
if and only if
a(E,X,\Delta)\geq\varepsilon
). Then in any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP, the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor is at most

\left(\left\lceil\frac{e_{+}(X,\Delta)+3}{\varepsilon}\right\rceil!\right)^{2^{1+e_{+}(X,\Delta)}}.

Proof.

Let
(X,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
be a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP. We first prove that
\mathrm{mld}(X,\Delta)\geq\varepsilon
. In fact, by passing to the terminalization, it suffices to prove this when
(X,\Delta)
is terminal. Write
\Delta=\sum b_{i}B_{i}
where the
B_{i}
are irreducible components of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
, then
\mathrm{mld}(X,\Delta)=1-\max_{i}\{b_{i}\}
. On the other hand, if we blow up a prime divisor in the smooth locus of
B_{i}
, we get an exceptional divisor
E
with
a(E,X,\Delta)=1-b_{i}>0
, thus as
\varepsilon
is the smallest positive discrepancy we have
1-b_{i}\geq\varepsilon
. This gives
\mathrm{mld}(X,\Delta)\geq\varepsilon
and hence we also have
\mathrm{mld}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\geq\varepsilon
.

Let
e:=e_{+}(X,\Delta)
. By definition, every divisor
E
over
X
with
a(E,X,\Delta)<\varepsilon
also satisfies
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq 0
. As
(X,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP, we have
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
, hence any divisor
E
with
a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})<\varepsilon
also satisfies
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq 0
. If
E
is exceptional over
X^{\prime}
, then either it is also exceptional over
X
, or it is a component of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
. It follows that there are at most
e
exceptional divisors
E
over
X^{\prime}
with
a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})<\varepsilon
; in particular, there are at most
e
divisors
E
with
0<a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})<\varepsilon
(all such divisors are necessarily exceptional over
X^{\prime}
). Since we also have
e(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\leq e_{+}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\leq e_{+}(X,\Delta)=e
and
\mathrm{mld}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\geq\varepsilon
, Lemma
3.21
implies that the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor on
X^{\prime}
is at most

\left(\left\lceil\frac{e+2}{\varepsilon}\right\rceil!\right)^{2^{e}}\cdot\left(\left\lceil\frac{3}{\varepsilon}\right\rceil!\right)^{2^{e-1}}\leq\left(\left\lceil\frac{e+3}{\varepsilon}\right\rceil!\right)^{2^{e+1}}.

∎

4.
Boundedness of fibers

In this section, we improve the argument from the previous section to prove the boundedness of the fibers of the extremal contractions and flips in the MMP.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial klt pair and
X\rightarrow T
a projective morphism. Assume that
K_{X}+\Delta
or
\Delta
is big over
T
. Then there exists a projective family
\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{B}
over a finite type base
\mathcal{B}
, such that in any sequence of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
T
, every fiber of the extremal contractions or the flips is isomorphic to
\mathcal{W}_{b}
for some
b\in\mathcal{B}
.

Recall that a projective morphism
f\colon(X,\Delta)\to Z
is called an extremal contraction if it is the contraction of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-negative extremal ray. There are three types of extremal contractions: divisorial contractions, flipping contractions, and Mori fiber spaces.

4.1.
Strategy

Our strategy towards Theorem
4.1
is to first prove that the germs of the extremal contractions and the flips are bounded up to special degenerations, and then analyze the possible deformations to show the boundedness of their fibers. For the special boundedness step, we shall prove the following statement.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varepsilon>0
. Let
\mathcal{S}
be the set of Fano type fibration germs
X\to Z\ni z
with
\dim X=n
such that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)\geq\varepsilon
for some
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
0\leq\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}-K_{X}
. Then
\mathcal{S}
is bounded up to Fano type degenerations (Definition
2.36
).

Observe that the Fano type assumption implies that
\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}-K_{X}
is big over
Z
. As
Z
is affine (by the definition of a fibration germ), the
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Delta
is in fact big, thus its log canonical volume
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)
is well-defined.

Using similar arguments, we also prove the following more general result on log Calabi-Yau fibration germs, which is in turn a variant of
[
32
]
*Conjecture 8.9 and
[
27
]
*Theorem 1.3.

Theorem 4.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varepsilon,\delta>0
. Let
\mathcal{S}
be the set of log Calabi-Yau fibration germs
(X,\Delta)\to Z\ni z
such that

(1)

\dim X=n
, and all the nonzero coefficients of
\Delta
are at least
\delta
,

(2)

X
is of Fano type over
Z
, and

(3)

\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)\geq\varepsilon
(Definition
3.9
).

Then
\mathcal{S}
is log specially bounded (Definition
2.36
).

Section
4.2
–
4.5
are devoted to the proof of Theorems
4.2
and
4.3
. Before we delve into this somewhat lengthy proof, we first explain the deformation step in the proof of Theorem
4.1
(assuming Theorem
4.2
). This is done through the following auxiliary result.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a set of Fano type fibration germs that is bounded up to Fano type degenerations. Then the set

\mathcal{T}=\{f^{-1}(z)\,|\,f\colon X\to Z\ni z\mbox{ belongs to }\mathcal{S}\}

is bounded.

Proof.

By assumption, there exists a bounded family
\mathcal{S}_{0}
of Fano type fibrations such that any fibration germ
f\colon X\to Z\ni z
in
\mathcal{S}
degenerates to some
f_{0}\colon X_{0}\to Z_{0}\ni z_{0}
in
\mathcal{S}_{0}
by a Fano type test configuration
\widetilde{f}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
. Since
\mathcal{S}_{0}
is bounded, there exist some positive integers
r
and
N
depending only on
\mathcal{S}_{0}
, some very ample line bundle
L_{0}
on
X_{0}
, and a surjective homomorphism

\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\mathcal{O}_{Z_{0}}^{\oplus N}\to f_{0*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}}(L_{0}),

such that the induced map

\mu\colon\mathrm{Sym}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\to\mathcal{R}_{0}:=\oplus_{m\in\mathbb{N}}f_{0*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}}(mL_{0})

of graded
\mathcal{O}_{Z_{0}}
-algebras is also surjective, and its kernel
\mathcal{G}
is generated by homogeneous elements of degree at most
r
. In particular, the linear system
|L_{0}|
induces a projectively normal embedding
X_{0}\hookrightarrow Z_{0}\times\mathbb{P}^{N-1}
and
X_{0}
is scheme-theoretically cut out by equations of degree at most
r
in
Z_{0}\times\mathbb{P}^{N}
.

Since
\mathcal{X}
is of Fano type over
\mathcal{Z}
, we have
R^{1}\widetilde{f}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}=R^{2}\widetilde{f}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}=0
by Kawamata-Viehweg vanishing, thus from the long exact sequence associated to the exponential exact sequence
0\to\mathbb{Z}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{exp}}\mathcal{O}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}\to 0
we see that over some sufficiently small analytic neighborhood
U\subseteq\mathcal{Z}
of
z_{0}
we have

\mathrm{Pic}(\widetilde{f}^{-1}(U))\cong H^{2}(\widetilde{f}^{-1}(U),\mathbb{Z})\cong H^{2}(\widetilde{f}^{-1}(z_{0}),\mathbb{Z})

where the last isomorphism comes from a deformation retraction of
\widetilde{f}^{-1}(U)
to
\widetilde{f}^{-1}(z_{0})
. Similarly, as
\mathcal{X}_{0}
is of Fano type over
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
, there exists some sufficiently small analytic neighborhood
V\subseteq Z_{0}
of
z_{0}
such that

\mathrm{Pic}(f_{0}^{-1}(V))\cong H^{2}(f_{0}^{-1}(z_{0}),\mathbb{Z})=H^{2}(\widetilde{f}^{-1}(z_{0}),\mathbb{Z}).

It follows that
L_{0}
extends to a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
over some small analytic neighborhood
U
of
z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}
. Possibly shrinking
U
, we may assume that
\mathcal{L}
is ample over
U
.

By Kawamata-Viehweg vanishing, we have
R^{1}\widetilde{f}_{*}\mathcal{L}^{m}=0
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus the long exact sequence associated to
0\to\mathcal{L}^{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t}}{{\to}}\mathcal{L}^{m}\to L_{0}^{m}\to 0
(where
t
is the linear coordinate on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
) gives

(4.1)

0\to\widetilde{f}_{*}\mathcal{L}^{m}|_{U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t}}{{\to}}\widetilde{f}_{*}\mathcal{L}^{m}|_{U}\to f_{0*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}}(mL_{0})|_{U}\to 0

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
\mathcal{L}
is ample over
U
, the
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-algebra

\mathcal{R}:=\oplus_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\widetilde{f}_{*}\mathcal{L}^{m}|_{U}
is finitely generated. Recall that
\mathcal{R}_{0}
is generated by at most
N
homogeneous elements of degree
1
by our choice of
L_{0}
. Combined with (
4.1
) and Nakayama’s lemma we deduce that
\mathcal{R}
is also generated (as an
\mathcal{O}_{U}
-algebra) by at most
N
homogeneous elements of degree
1
. More precisely, on the open set
U
by (
4.1
) we may lift the homomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{0}|_{U}\to f_{0*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{0}}(L_{0})|_{U}
to a homomorphism

\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{O}_{U}^{\oplus N}\to\widetilde{f}_{*}\mathcal{L}|_{U},

and for any positive integer
m
, after possibly shrinking
U
, the induced homomorphism

\widetilde{\mu}\colon\mathrm{Sym}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathcal{R}

is surjective in degree
\leq m
by Nakayama’s lemma. Since
\mathcal{R}
is generated by finitely many homogeneous elements, we see that
\widetilde{\mu}
is surjective after shrinking
U
. In particular,
we have a closed embedding
\widetilde{f}^{-1}(U)\hookrightarrow U\times\mathbb{P}^{N-1}
. Let
\mathcal{F}=\ker(\widetilde{\mu})
and recall
\mathcal{G}=\ker(\mu)
. By construction,
\mu
is the reduction of
\widetilde{\mu}
modulo
t
. Note that
\mathcal{R}
is torsion free and hence flat over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. It follows that the exact sequence
0\to\mathcal{F}\to\mathrm{Sym}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathcal{R}\to 0
remains exact modulo
t
and hence
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{F}/t\mathcal{F}
. Therefore, as
\mathcal{G}
is generated by homogeneous elements of degree at most
r
, the same holds for
\mathcal{F}
by another application of Nakayama’s lemma as before. Thus
\widetilde{f}^{-1}(U)
is cut out by equations of degree at most
r
in
U\times\mathbb{P}^{N-1}
and hence every fiber
\widetilde{f}^{-1}(u)
, where
u\in U
, is cut out by equations of degree at most
r
in
\mathbb{P}^{N-1}
.

Since
\widetilde{f}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
is a test configuration of
f\colon X\to Z\ni z
, for any open neighborhood
U
of
z_{0}\in\mathcal{Z}
there exists some
u\in U
such that
\widetilde{f}^{-1}(u)
is isomorphic to
f^{-1}(z)
. Therefore, every member of
\mathcal{T}
can be cut out by equations of degree at most
r
in
\mathbb{P}^{N-1}
and hence
\mathcal{T}
is bounded.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.1
assuming Theorem
4.2
.

By Lemma
2.18
, there exists some big
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Delta_{0}\geq 0
on
X
such that
(X,\Delta_{0})
is klt and every
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
T
is also a
(K_{X}+\Delta_{0})
-MMP. Replacing
\Delta
by
\Delta_{0}
, we see that it suffices to prove the theorem when
\Delta
is big.

Suppose that
(X,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(Y,\Delta_{Y})
is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP and
f\colon(Y,\Delta_{Y})\to Z
is an extremal contraction over
T
. By Lemma
3.11
, we have
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{Y}(\Delta_{Y})\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)>0
. Since
-(K_{Y}+\Delta_{Y})
is ample over
Z
, for any closed point
z\in Z
we can choose some
0\leq H\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}-(K_{Y}+\Delta_{Y})
such that
(Y,\Delta_{Y}+H)\to Z\ni z
is a log Calabi-Yau fibration germ (implicitly, here we replace
Z
by an affine neighborhood of
z
in order to talk about the fibration germ). By Lemma
3.15
, we also have
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{Y}(\Delta_{Y}+H)\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)
. By Theorem
4.2
, the Fano type fibration germ
Y\to Z\ni z
belongs to a set depending only on
\dim X
and
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)
that is bounded up to Fano type degenerations. By Lemma
4.4
, we conclude that the fiber
f^{-1}(z)
of the extremal contraction belongs to a bounded family depending only on
(X,\Delta)
.

Next suppose that
(Y,\Delta_{Y})\to Z
is a small contraction and
g\colon(Y^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to Z
is the flip. Let
H^{\prime}
be the strict transform of
H
on
Y^{\prime}
. Then by Lemma
3.11
and the above discussion we have
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{Y^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime}+H^{\prime})\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{Y}(\Delta_{Y}+H)\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)
. Thus the same argument above applies to the fibration
Y^{\prime}\to Z\ni z
and we conclude that the fiber
g^{-1}(z)
of the flip also belongs to a bounded family depending only on
(X,\Delta)
.
∎

4.2.
Relative cone construction

We now turn to the proofs of Theorems
4.2
and
4.3
. We first consider a special boundedness result for log Fano fibrations with a fixed finite set of rational coefficients.

Theorem 4.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\varepsilon>0
, and let
I\subseteq[0,1]\cap\mathbb{Q}
be a finite set. Let
\mathcal{S}
be the set of log Fano fibration germs
(X,\Delta)\to Z\ni z
with
\dim X=n
,
\mathrm{Coeff}(\Delta)\subseteq I
such that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(H)\geq\varepsilon
for some
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
H\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}-(K_{X}+\Delta)
. Then
\mathcal{S}
is specially bounded (Definition
2.36
).

When
X=Z
, this essentially follows from (by Lemma
3.12
) the special boundedness of klt singularities proved in
[
65
]
. Combined with the argument from the previous subsection, Theorem
4.5
already implies the rational coefficient case of Theorem
4.1
for fibers of the extremal contractions.

The main idea of the proof is to consider the relative cone of the log Fano fibration and reduce to
[
65
]
after analyzing the local volume of the cone singularity. Before we do so, we make a digression to discuss some generality of the relative cone construction. Many results in this subsection are parallel to those of
[
47
]
*Section 3.1, which deals with cones over projective pairs.

We start with some general setup and introduce some notation that will be fixed throughout this subsection. Let
f\colon X\to Z
be a fibration and let
L
be an
f
-ample
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor on
X
. Let

\mathcal{R}_{m}:=f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(mL)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathcal{R}:=\oplus_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{R}_{m}.

We are interested in the relative (orbifold) cone
Y:=\mathrm{Spec}_{Z}(\mathcal{R})
. Next, let

\widetilde{Y}:=\mathrm{Spec}_{X}\left(\oplus_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(mL)\right)

and let
\pi\colon\widetilde{Y}\to X
be the induced morphism. It has a section
E\subseteq\widetilde{Y}
defined by the ideal
\oplus_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(mL)
. The morphism
\widetilde{Y}\setminus E\to X
is a Seifert
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle (in the sense of
[
45
]
) and
E
can be identified with the zero section. In particular, the divisor
E
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and
E\cong X
. Locally on
Z
we have
\mathcal{R}=H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y}})
(in other words
(f\circ\pi)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y}}=\mathcal{R}
). Hence we have an induced morphism
\varphi\colon\widetilde{Y}\to Y
over
Z
which is an isomorphism away from the zero section
E
(by the ampleness of
L
over
Z
), and
\varphi|_{E}=f
. Let
\Gamma:=\varphi(E)\cong Z
. It gives a section
\sigma\colon Z\to Y
of the projection
g\colon Y\to Z
. The situation is summarized by the following diagram.

(4.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

Note that if
f\colon X\to Z
is birational, then
\varphi
is small and induces an isomorphism
\varphi_{*}\colon\mathrm{Cl}(\widetilde{Y})\cong\mathrm{Cl}(Y)
. Otherwise
E
is the only exceptional divisor of
\varphi
and we get an isomorphism
\varphi_{*}\colon\mathrm{Cl}(\widetilde{Y})/[E]\cong\mathrm{Cl}(Y)
. We also have a natural pullback map
\pi^{*}\colon\mathrm{Cl}(X)\to\mathrm{Cl}(\widetilde{Y})
since
\pi
is equidimensional.

Lemma 4.6
.

The induced map
\pi^{*}\colon\mathrm{Cl}(X)\to\mathrm{Cl}(\widetilde{Y})
is an isomorphism. Moreover, we have
-E\sim\pi^{*}L
and
K_{\widetilde{Y}}+E\sim\pi^{*}K_{X}
.

Proof.

Over the smooth locus
U
of
X
the divisor
L
is Cartier, and
\pi^{-1}(U)
is an
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-bundle over
U
, hence
\pi^{*}
induces an isomorphism
\mathrm{Cl}(\pi^{-1}(U))\cong\mathrm{Cl}(U)
. Since
\pi
is equidimensional, the complement of
\pi^{-1}U
in
\widetilde{Y}
has codimension at least
2
, thus
\mathrm{Cl}(X)\cong\mathrm{Cl}(U)
and
\mathrm{Cl}(\widetilde{Y})\cong\mathrm{Cl}(\pi^{-1}(U))
. It follows that
\pi^{*}\colon\mathrm{Cl}(X)\to\mathrm{Cl}(\widetilde{Y})
is also an isomorphism.

Any rational section of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(L)\subseteq\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y}}
defines a rational function on
\widetilde{Y}
and induces the linear equivalence relation
\pi^{*}L+E\sim 0
. If
z
is the affine coordinate of the fiber of
\pi^{-1}(U)\to U
, then
\frac{dz}{z}
is a nowhere zero section of
K_{\pi^{-1}(U)/U}+E
. This gives
K_{\pi^{-1}(U)/U}+E\sim 0
and hence
K_{\widetilde{Y}}+E\sim\pi^{*}K_{X}
as
\pi^{-1}(U)
(resp.
U
) is a big open subset of
\widetilde{Y}
(resp.
X
).
∎

For the next several lemmas, We say that the fibration
f\colon X\to Z
is
of fiber type
if
\dim X>\dim Z
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
G
be a Weil divisor on
X
. Then
\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}G
is Cartier if and only if either
f
is birational and
G\sim_{Z}0
, or
f
is of fiber type and
G\sim_{Z}mL
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover,
\pi^{*}G\sim_{Y}0
if and only if
G\sim_{Z}0
.

Proof.

First consider the case when
f
is birational. If
G=f^{*}G_{0}
for some Cartier divisor
G_{0}
on
Z
, then
\pi^{*}G\sim_{Y}0
and
\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}G=g^{*}G_{0}
is also Cartier. Conversely, if
G^{\prime}:=\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}G
is Cartier then
\pi^{*}G=\varphi^{*}G^{\prime}
as
\varphi
is small. Restricting to the zero section
E\cong X
gives
G=f^{*}\sigma^{*}G^{\prime}
, hence
G\sim_{Z}0
.

Next assume that
f
is of fiber type. The proof is similar. If
G-mL=f^{*}G_{0}
for some Cartier divisor
G_{0}
, then by Lemma
4.6
we have
\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}G=\varphi_{*}(\pi^{*}G+mE)\sim\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}(G-mL)=g^{*}G_{0}
, hence
\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}G
is also Cartier. Conversely, if
G^{\prime}:=\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}G
is Cartier then
\pi^{*}G+mE=\varphi^{*}G^{\prime}
for some integer
m
. If in addition
\pi^{*}G\sim_{Y}0
then
m=0
. Restricting to the zero section
E\cong X
and noting that
-E|_{E}\cong L
by Lemma
4.6
, we obtain
G-mL\sim f^{*}\sigma^{*}G^{\prime}
, hence
G\sim_{Z}mL
.
∎

Next let
\Delta\geq 0
be an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
and consider the
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
D:=\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}\Delta\geq 0
on
Y
. Geometrically, it is the relative cone over
\Delta
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Assume that
f
is birational and
0<\lambda\leq 1
(resp.
0\leq\lambda\leq 1
). Then the pair
(Y,D+(1-\lambda)\Gamma)
is klt (resp. log canonical) if and only if
K_{X}+\Delta+\lambda L\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}0
and
(X,\Delta)
is klt (resp. log canonical).

Proof.

We only treat the klt case since the log canonical version is similar. By Lemma
4.6
, we have

K_{Y}+D+(1-\lambda)\Gamma\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{*}(K_{\widetilde{Y}}+\pi^{*}\Delta+(1-\lambda)E)\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+\lambda L).

Thus by Lemma
4.7
, we see that
K_{Y}+D+(1-\lambda)\Gamma
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier if and only if
K_{X}+\Delta+\lambda L\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}0
, in which case
(\widetilde{Y},\pi^{*}\Delta+(1-\lambda)E)
is the crepant pullback of
(Y,D+(1-\lambda)\Gamma)
.

By
[
45
]
*Paragraphs 40-42, if
(Y,D+(1-\lambda)\Gamma)
is klt, then so is
(X,\Delta)
since the latter is the orbifold quotient of
(Y\setminus\Gamma,D|_{Y\setminus\Gamma})
by the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. Conversely, we show that
(Y,D+(1-\lambda)\Gamma)
is klt if
(X,\Delta)
is klt. To see this, it suffices to show that the crepant pullback
(\widetilde{Y},\pi^{*}\Delta+(1-\lambda)E)
is klt. Since

(K_{\widetilde{Y}}+\pi^{*}\Delta+E)|_{E}\cong K_{X}+\Delta
(where we identify
E
with
X
) and
(X,\Delta)
is klt, by inversion of adjunction we see that
(\widetilde{Y},\pi^{*}\Delta+E)
is plt in a neighborhood of
E
. Since the natural
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\widetilde{Y}
preserves the pair
(\widetilde{Y},\pi^{*}\Delta+E)
, every non-klt center intersects the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-fixed locus
E
; this implies that
(\widetilde{Y},\pi^{*}\Delta+E)
is plt everywhere. As
0<\lambda\leq 1
, we see that
(\widetilde{Y},\pi^{*}\Delta+(1-\lambda)E)
is klt.
∎

By essentially the same argument, we also have the following.

Lemma 4.9
.

Assume that
f
is of fiber type. Then the pair
(Y,D)
is klt (resp. log canonical) if and only if
K_{X}+\Delta+\lambda L\sim_{\mathbb{Q},Z}0
for some
\lambda>0
(resp.
\lambda\geq 0
) and
(X,\Delta)
is klt (resp. log canonical). In such case we have
A_{Y,D}(E)=\lambda
.

Proof.

Since
K_{Y}+D=\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)
, the first part follows from Lemma
4.7
as in the proof of Lemma
4.9
. It remains to show that
K_{X}+\Delta+\lambda L\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}0
where
\lambda:=A_{Y,D}(E)
. But this again follows from Lemma
4.7
as
\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta+\lambda L)\sim_{\mathbb{R}}K_{\widetilde{Y}}+\pi^{*}\Delta+(1-\lambda)E=\varphi^{*}(K_{Y}+D)\sim_{\mathbb{R},Y}0
.
∎

4.3.
Volume estimate

We next apply the above cone construction to log Fano fibrations and prove the key local volume estimate towards the proof of Theorem
4.5
.

Let
f\colon(X,\Delta)\to Z\ni z
be a log Fano fibration germ. We assume that the coefficients of
\Delta
are rational. Let
r
be a positive integer such that
r\Delta
has integer coefficients, let

\mathcal{R}=\oplus_{m\in\mathbb{N}}H^{0}(X,-mr(K_{X}+\Delta)),

and let
Y=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{R})
be the relative cone with respect to the Weil divisor
L:=-r(K_{X}+\Delta)
. We keep the notation from the diagram (
4.2
). Let

(4.3)

D=\begin{cases}\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}\Delta+\left(1-\frac{1}{r}\right)\Gamma,&\quad\mbox{if }f\mbox{ is birational},\\
\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}\Delta,&\quad\mbox{if }f\mbox{ is of fiber type}.\end{cases}

Note that
\Gamma
is a divisor on
Y
if and only if
f
is birational.
By Lemmas
4.8
and
4.9
, the pair
(Y,D)
is klt. Let
y=\sigma(z)\in Y
. The main result of this subsection is the following.

Theorem 4.10
.

Under the above setup, we have

\widehat{\rm vol}(y,Y,D)\geq\frac{\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X,\Delta}(H)}{r}

for any
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
H\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}-(K_{X}+\Delta)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.16
, and since
\Delta
has rational coefficient, it is enough to prove the statement when
H
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor. Since the question is local on
Z
, we may also shrink
Z
around
z
and assume that
H\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}-(K_{X}+\Delta)
. By definition, it suffices to show that

\widehat{\rm vol}(y,Y,D)\geq\frac{(1-\varepsilon)^{n}\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})}{r}

for any
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
and any admissible graded linear series
V_{\bullet}
of
H
. Our plan is to find a sequence of subspaces
W_{m}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}
of increasing dimensions such that

\min\{\mathrm{lct}_{y}(s)\,|\,0\neq s\in W_{m}\}

are reasonably bounded from below and apply Lemma
3.4
. Here we use the shorthand notation
\mathrm{lct}_{y}(s)
for
\mathrm{lct}_{y}(Y,D;\{s=0\})
where
0\neq s\in\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}
. In the simplest case, if
-r(K_{X}+\Delta)
is Cartier and
H=-(K_{X}+\Delta)
, then each
V_{mr}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) is already a subspace of
\mathcal{R}_{m}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}
, and we can take
W_{m}
to be the span of
V_{r},V_{2r},\cdots,V_{mr}
.

In general, let
k
be a sufficiently divisible positive integer such that
kH
and
k(K_{X}+\Delta)
are both Cartier,
kH\sim-k(K_{X}+\Delta)
, and
V_{km}\neq 0
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
V^{\prime}_{\bullet}
be the graded linear series of
kH
given by
V^{\prime}_{m}=V_{km}
. Let
\alpha=\frac{1-\varepsilon}{k}
. Since
H\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}-(K_{X}+\Delta)
is ample over
Z
, so is

-mr(K_{X}+\Delta)-\lfloor\alpha mr\rfloor\cdot kH\sim-(mr-\lfloor\alpha mr\rfloor\cdot k)(K_{X}+\Delta)

for any
m>0
; moreover, for any sufficiently positive integer
m
(say, when
m\geq m_{0}
) we have
mr-\lfloor\alpha mr\rfloor\cdot k\geq\varepsilon mr\gg 0
and thus by Lemma
2.9
we can find some

u_{m}\in H^{0}(X,-mr(K_{X}+\Delta)-\lfloor\alpha mr\rfloor\cdot kH)

such that
(X,\Delta+\frac{1}{\varepsilon mr}\{u_{m}=0\})
is log canonical.

Multiplication by
u_{m}
takes
V^{\prime}_{\lfloor\alpha mr\rfloor}
into
\mathcal{R}_{m}=H^{0}(X,-mr(K_{X}+\Delta))\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}
and we take
W_{m}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}
to be the span of
u_{j}\cdot V^{\prime}_{\lfloor\alpha jr\rfloor}
for
j=m_{0},\dots,m
. We claim that

(4.4)

m\cdot\mathrm{lct}_{y}(s)\geq\frac{1}{r}

for all
0\neq s\in W_{m}
. By the following Lemma
4.11
, since
W_{m}
is invariant under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
Y
and has a weight decomposition
W_{m}=\oplus_{j=m_{0}}^{m}u_{j}\cdot V^{\prime}_{\lfloor\alpha jr\rfloor}
, it suffices to prove the claim when
s=u_{j}\cdot w
for some
m_{0}\leq j\leq m
and some
w\in V^{\prime}_{\lfloor\alpha jr\rfloor}
.

Since
V_{\bullet}
is admissible, we know that the pair
(X,\Delta+\frac{1}{\alpha jkr}\{w=0\})=(X,\Delta+\frac{1}{(1-\varepsilon)jr}\{w=0\})
is log canonical. By construction
(X,\Delta+\frac{1}{\varepsilon jr}\{u_{j}=0\})
is also log canonical. By taking convex combination, we deduce that

\left(X,\Delta+(1-\varepsilon)\cdot\frac{1}{(1-\varepsilon)jr}\{w=0\}+\varepsilon\cdot\frac{1}{\varepsilon jr}\{u_{j}=0\}\right)

is log canonical. In other words, the pair

(X,\Delta+\Delta_{0}):=\left(X,\Delta+\frac{1}{jr}\{u_{j}w=0\}\right)

is log canonical. On the other hand, we have
K_{X}+\Delta+\Delta_{0}\sim_{\mathbb{Q},Z}0
as
u_{j}w\in H^{0}(X,-jr(K_{X}+\Delta))
. If
D_{0}:=\frac{1}{jr}\{s=0\}
is the corresponding divisor on
Y
, then we also have

D_{0}=\begin{cases}\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}\Delta_{0}+\frac{1}{r}\Gamma&\quad\mbox{if }f\mbox{ is birational},\\
\varphi_{*}\pi^{*}\Delta_{0}&\quad\mbox{if }f\mbox{ is of fiber type}.\end{cases}

By Lemma
4.8
and
4.9
, these imply that
(Y,D+D_{0})
is log canonical at
y
, which gives
\mathrm{lct}_{y}(s)\geq\frac{1}{jr}\geq\frac{1}{mr}
and hence the claim (
4.4
).

By Lemma
2.8
we have

\dim W_{m}=\sum_{j=m_{0}}^{m}\dim V^{\prime}_{\lfloor\alpha jr\rfloor}=\sum_{j=m_{0}}^{m}\dim V_{\lfloor\alpha jr\rfloor\cdot k}=(\alpha kr)^{n}\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})\frac{m^{n+1}}{(n+1)!}+o(m^{n+1}).

Combined with (
4.4
) and Lemma
3.4
we see that

\widehat{\rm vol}(y,Y,D)\geq\frac{c_{1}}{r^{n+1}}

for any
c_{1}<(\alpha kr)^{n}\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})
. It follows that

\widehat{\rm vol}(y,Y,D)\geq\frac{(\alpha k)^{n}\cdot\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})}{r}=\frac{(1-\varepsilon)^{n}\mathrm{vol}(V_{\bullet})}{r}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

The following lemma is used in the above proof. We keep the same notation
\mathrm{lct}_{y}(s)
for
\mathrm{lct}_{y}(Y,D;\{s=0\})

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
y\in(Y,D)
be a klt singularity with a torus
\mathbb{T}
-action and let
V\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}
be a finite dimensional
\mathbb{T}
-invariant subspace. Assume that for some constant
c\geq 0
we have
\mathrm{lct}_{y}(s)\geq c
for all nonzero
\mathbb{T}
-eigenvectors
s\in V
. Then
\mathrm{lct}_{y}(s)\geq c
for all nonzero
s\in V
.

Proof.

For any
0\neq s\in V
, there exists some one-parameter subgroup
\rho\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{T}
such that the flat limit
D_{0}
of
\rho(t)\cdot D
, where
D=\{s=0\}
and when
t\to 0
, is a
\mathbb{T}
-invariant divisor. Indeed, if
s=\sum_{i=1}^{m}s_{i}
is the weight decomposition where
s_{i}
has weight
\alpha_{i}\in\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{G}_{m})
, and choose

\rho
such that
\langle\rho,\alpha_{1}\rangle<\cdots<\langle\rho,\alpha_{m}\rangle
, then the flat limit is
\{s_{1}=0\}
. By assumption, we have
\mathrm{lct}_{y}(D_{0})\geq c
, hence by the lower semi-continuity of log canonical threshold
[
6
]
*Corollary 1.10, we get
\mathrm{lct}_{y}(D)\geq c
as well.
∎

By
[
65
]
and the stable degeneration theorem (see Theorem
2.27
), we know that klt singularities whose local volumes are bounded away from zero are bounded up to special degeneration. Combined with Theorem
4.10
, we are now ready to prove Theorem
4.5
.

Proof of Theorem
4.5
.

Choose some positive integer
r
such that
I\subseteq\frac{1}{r}\mathbb{N}
. Let
f\colon(X,\Delta)\to Z\ni z
be a log Fano fibration germ in
\mathcal{S}
. Let
y\in(Y,D)
be the relative cone over
(X,\Delta)
with respect to the ample Weil divisor
L=-r(K_{X}+\Delta)
, where the boundary
D
is defined as in (
4.3
). By Theorem
4.10
and our assumption, we have
\widehat{\rm vol}(y,Y,D)\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{r}
. Note that
y\in(Y,D)
carries a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action by the cone construction.

By Theorem
2.27
, there exists an
\mathbb{R}
-Gorenstein family
\mathcal{B}\subseteq(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{B}
of klt singularities, depending only on
n,I
and
\varepsilon
, with the action of an algebraic torus
\mathbb{T}
over
\mathcal{B}
, such that
y\in(Y,D)
admits a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant special degeneration to
b\in(\mathcal{Y}_{b},\mathcal{D}_{b})
for some
b\in\mathcal{B}
, and by specializing the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action to
b\in(\mathcal{Y}_{b},\mathcal{D}_{b})
, we get a one-parameter subgroup of
\mathbb{T}_{b}
.

By base change, we shall assume that on each of the connected components
\mathcal{B}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{B}_{k}
of
\mathcal{B}
, we have
\mathbb{T}=\mathbb{T}_{i}\times\mathcal{B}
for some torus
\mathbb{T}_{i}
. By Lemma
2.23
, the weight monoid
S_{i}
is constant over each
\mathcal{B}_{i}
. We claim that for each
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
, there are only finitely many one-parameter subgroups of
\mathbb{T}_{i}
that are possibly induced by the above construction. To see this, suppose that
b\in\mathcal{B}_{i}
(so we may identify
\mathbb{T}_{b}
with
\mathbb{T}_{i}
) and let
E
be the divisor over
Y
as in the diagram (
4.2
); that is, the zero section of the corresponding Seifert
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle over
Z
. Then we have
\mathrm{wt}_{\rho}=\mathrm{ord}_{E}
as valuations on
Y
, where we denote by
\rho\colon\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathrm{Aut}(Y,D)
the one-parameter subgroup coming from the cone construction. We can identify the induced one-parameter subgroup of
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{Y}_{b},\mathcal{D}_{b})
with some primitive element of
S_{i}^{\vee}
, which we still denote by
\rho
. By (
4.3
) and Lemma
4.9
, we know that
A_{Y,D}(E)=\frac{1}{r}
, thus by Lemma
2.24
we deduce that
A_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{wt}_{\rho})=A_{\mathcal{Y}_{b},\mathcal{D}_{b}}(\mathrm{wt}_{\rho})=\frac{1}{r}
. Since the log discrepancy function
\xi\mapsto A_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{wt}_{\xi})
is positive (as
(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{D})
is klt) and linear on
S_{i}^{\vee}
(by Lemma
2.21
), we see that there are at most finitely many
\rho\in S_{i}^{\vee}
such that
A_{\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{D}}(\mathrm{wt}_{\rho})=\frac{1}{r}
. This proves the claim.

Suppose that
y\in(Y,D)
specially and
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariantly degenerates to
b\in(\mathcal{Y}_{b},\mathcal{D}_{b})
. By the following Lemma
4.12
, it induces a special degeneration of
(X,\Delta)\to Z\ni z
to the log Fano fibration germ
(\mathcal{Y}_{b}\setminus\mathcal{Y}_{b}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}},\mathcal{D}_{b}\setminus\mathcal{D}_{b}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}})/\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathcal{Y}_{b}/\!\!/\mathbb{G}_{m}\ni z_{b}
, where
z_{b}
is the image of
b\in\mathcal{Y}_{b}
in the GIT quotient. Thus it remains to show that latter form an
\mathbb{R}
-Gorenstein family of log Fano fibrations over a finite type base. This is clear since there are only finitely many one-parameter subgroups of
\mathbb{T}_{i}
to consider and
\mathcal{B}
is of finite type.
∎

Lemma 4.12
.

Let
(X,\Delta)\to Z\ni z
be a log Fano fibration germ and let
y\in(Y,D)
be the relative cone with respect to
L=-r(K_{X}+\Delta)
as above for some
r\in\mathbb{N}
such that
r\Delta
has integer coefficients. Let
(\mathcal{Y}=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{R}),\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
be a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant special test configuration of the singularity
y\in(Y,D)
. Then it induces a special test configuration

(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{Z}

of
(X,\Delta)\to Z\ni z
such that
(\mathcal{Y},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})
is the relative cone of
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})
over
\mathcal{Z}
with respect to
\mathcal{L}:=-r(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})
.

Proof.

Since
(\mathcal{Y},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant test configuration, we have a weight decomposition
\mathcal{R}=\oplus_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{R}_{m}
with respect to the fiberwise
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. Note that there are no negative weights by Lemma
2.23
. Let
\mathcal{X}:=\mathrm{Proj}(\mathcal{R})
and
\mathcal{Z}:=\mathrm{Spec}(\mathcal{R}_{0})
. Then the natural morphism
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
over
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is a test configuration of the fibration
X\to Z
. Since
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is a test configuration of the singularity
y\in Y
, the closure
C
of
\{y\}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
in
\mathcal{Y}
is a section of the morphism
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
. The latter factors through
\mathcal{Z}
, hence the closure of
\{z\}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
in
\mathcal{Z}
coincides with the image of
C
and is a section of
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
. This implies that
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is also a test configuration of the singularity
z\in Z
and therefore
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
is a test configuration of the fibration germ
X\to Z\ni z
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be the closure of
\Delta\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
in
\mathcal{X}
. We next show that the Weil divisor
\mathcal{L}=-r(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})
is ample
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and
(\mathcal{Y},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})
is the relative orbifold cone of
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})
over
\mathcal{Z}
respect to
\mathcal{L}
. Let
\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{Y}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
be the fixed locus of the fiberwise
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action. Then
\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{X}
is a Seifert
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle. We claim that it has reduced (hence smooth) fibers over all codimension one points of
\mathcal{X}
. In fact, away from
\mathcal{X}_{0}
the Seifert
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle is given by the Weil divisor
-r(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})
, hence the codimension one fibers are reduced by
[
45
]
*Theorem 7. The fiber over the generic point of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
is also reduced since
(\mathcal{Y},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is a special test configuration and in particular
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
is reduced. This proves the claim. By
[
45
]
*Theorem 7, we deduce that the Seifert
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle
\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{W}\to\mathcal{X}
is given by some Weil divisor
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathcal{X}
. We necessarily have
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}(1)
and thus
\mathcal{L}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and ample. By construction,
\mathcal{L}|_{\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}_{0}}\sim-r(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})|_{\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}_{0}}
. Since
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
is integral, so is
\mathcal{X}_{0}
. As
\mathcal{X}_{0}\sim 0
, this implies that
\mathcal{L}\sim-r(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})
and therefore
-r(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier and ample. Replacing
\mathcal{L}
by
-r(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})
, we conclude that
(\mathcal{Y},\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})
is the relative orbifold cone of
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})
over
\mathcal{Z}
with respect to
\mathcal{L}=-r(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})
. Since
(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{Y}_{0}+\Delta_{\mathcal{Y}})
is plt by assumption, the base
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{X}_{0}+\mathcal{D})
is also plt by
[
45
]
*Paragraphs 40-42 as in the proof of Lemma
4.8
. This implies that
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{Z}
is a special test configuration and we finish the proof.
∎

4.4.
Finiteness of models

To go from the special boundedness of log Fano fibration germs in Theorem
4.5
to the log special boundedness of Fano type fibration germs in Theorems
4.2
and
4.3
, we employ the following proof strategy. For simplicity, suppose we are in the setting of Theorem
4.2
. Let
X\to Z\ni z
be a Fano type fibration germ. Then the ample model
X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
of
-K_{X}
exists over
Z
. Assume that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)\geq\varepsilon
for some
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
0\leq\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}-K_{X}
as in Theorem
4.2
. From Lemma
3.11
, it is not hard to see that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})\geq\varepsilon
where
\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}-K_{X^{\prime}}
is the strict transform of
\Delta
. Since
X^{\prime}\to Z\ni z
is a log Fano fibration germ, it belongs to a specially bounded set by Theorem
4.5
. Next we observe that
X
can be recovered from
X^{\prime}
up to finite ambiguity. In fact, all the exceptional divisors of the birational map
X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
have non-positive discrepancies over
X^{\prime}
. Since
X^{\prime}
is log Fano over
Z
, there are only finitely many birational models
X
with this property. With this in mind, we prove a family version of such finiteness of models (Theorem
4.13
). As a result, if
X^{\prime}\to Z
belongs to a bounded (resp. specially bounded) set, then so is
X\to Z
(Theorem
4.22
).

Turning to more details, we first prove in this subsection the following statement that roughly says a set of Fano type fibrations is bounded if their ample models are bounded. The stronger version, where we replace boundedness by special boundedness, will be established in the next subsection.

Theorem 4.13
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a bounded set of log Fano fibrations (Definition
2.32
). Then there exists a bounded set
\mathcal{T}
of Fano type fibrations such that for any
(X,\Delta)\to Z
in
\mathcal{S}
and any birational map
f\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X
over
Z
such that
f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)=K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}
for some
\Delta^{\prime}\geq 0
, we have
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to Z
belongs to
\mathcal{T}
.

This can be viewed as a local version of
[
27
]
*Section 2.1 and we essentially mimic the strategy of
loc. cit.
. The first step is to prove a local version of
[
19
]
*Lemma 6.6. Namely, given a family of Fano type fibrations, the relative Picard group is a constant local system possibly after a base change (Corollary
4.17
). For this we need a few auxiliary results. For any fibration
f\colon X\to Y
, we let
N^{1}(X/Y):=\mathbb{Q}\otimes(\mathrm{Pic}(X)/f^{*}\mathrm{Pic}(Y))
, and let
H_{j}(X/Y,\mathbb{Q})\subseteq H_{j}(X,\mathbb{Q})
be the subspace generated by the images of

H_{j}(X_{y},\mathbb{Q})\to H_{j}(X,\mathbb{Q})
where
y\in Y
(
cf.

[
41
]
*Definition (12.1.2)). We also let
H_{i}(X/Y,\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{alg}}\subseteq H_{i}(X/Y,\mathbb{Q})
be the subspace generated by the algebraic cycles in the fiber of
f
.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a fibration. Assume that
R^{1}f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}=0
and
X
has rational singularities. Then the intersection pairing
N^{1}(X/Y)\times H_{2}(X/Y,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbb{Q}
induces an inclusion
\iota\colon N^{1}(X/Y)\hookrightarrow H_{2}(X/Y,\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{alg}}^{\vee}
. In particular,
\dim N^{1}(X/Y)<+\infty
. If in addition
X
is smooth, then
\iota
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The exponential exact sequence gives rise to a homomorphism
\mathrm{Pic}(X)\to H^{2}(X,\mathbb{Z})
and hence a natural pairing
\mathrm{Pic}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}\times H_{2}(X/Y,\mathbb{Q})\to H^{2}(X,\mathbb{Q})\times H_{2}(X,\mathbb{Q})\to\mathbb{Q}
. It descends to
N^{1}(X/Y)
as
\langle f^{*}\mathrm{Pic}(Y),H_{2}(X/Y,\mathbb{Q})\rangle=0
by definition. By
[
41
]
*Proposition (12.1.4), we see that the induced map
\iota
is an inclusion. Suppose that
X
is smooth and let
\overline{X}
be a smooth projective compactification of
X
. If
\iota
is not surjective, then there exists a nonzero algebraic class
\alpha\in H_{2}(X/Y,\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{alg}}
such that
(L\cdot\alpha)=0
for all line bundle
L
on
\overline{X}
. As
\overline{X}
is smooth, this implies that the image of
\alpha
in
H_{2}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Q})
is zero. But by (the dual statement of)
[
63
]
*Proposition 4.23, the composition
H_{2}(X/Y,\mathbb{Q})\hookrightarrow H_{2}(X,\mathbb{Q})\to H_{2}(\overline{X},\mathbb{Q})
is injective, a contradiction.
∎

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
f\colon X\to Y
be a fibration over a reduced base
T
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a coherent sheaf on
X
such that
R^{i}f_{*}\mathcal{F}=0
for some
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists a dense open subset
U
of
T
such that
R^{i}f_{*}\mathcal{F}_{t}=0
for all
t\in U
.

Proof.

By shrinking
T
we may assume that
T
is smooth. Since
R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{F}
is coherent for all
j
, by generic flatness we know that there exists a dense open subset
U\subseteq T
such that
R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{F}
is flat over
U
for all
j
. Similarly, after possibly shrinking
U
we may assume that
\mathcal{F}
is flat over
U
. In particular, for any smooth hypersurface
Z\subseteq U
with local defining equation
u
, the multiplication maps
\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{u}\mathcal{F}
and
R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{u}R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{F}
are injective for all
j
. From the long exact sequence, we deduce that
R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{Z}\cong R^{j}f_{*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{Z})
. By induction on the dimension of
U
, this implies that
R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{F}_{t}\cong R^{j}f_{*}\mathcal{F}\otimes k(t)
for all
j
. Thus as
R^{i}f_{*}\mathcal{F}=0
, we see that
R^{i}f_{*}\mathcal{F}_{t}=0
for all
t\in U
.
∎

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
\varphi\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
be a fibration over a variety
\mathcal{B}
. Assume that
\mathcal{X}
has rational singularities and
R^{1}\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}=R^{2}\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}=0
. Then there exists a generically finite dominant morphism
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
such that after base change
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=\mathcal{Z}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
, the restriction map

N^{1}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\mathcal{Z}^{\prime})\to N^{1}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{b})

is an isomorphism for every
b\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.15
and shrinking
\mathcal{B}
if necessary, we shall assume that
\mathcal{B}
is smooth,
\mathcal{X}_{b}
has rational singularities, and
R^{1}\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{b}}=R^{2}\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{b}}=0
for all
b\in\mathcal{B}
throughout the proof. First consider the special case when
\mathcal{X}
is smooth over
\mathcal{B}
. By Lemma
4.14
and
[
41
]
*Theorem (12.1.3), we have

N^{1}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Z})\cong H_{2}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{Q})_{\mathrm{alg}}^{\vee}\cong\mathrm{coker}(H^{2}(\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{Q})\to H^{2}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Q}))

and similarly

N^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}_{b})\cong\mathrm{coker}(H^{2}(\mathcal{Z}_{b},\mathbb{Q})\to H^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{Q})).

Denote the maps
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{B}
by
p
and
q
respectively. By
[
60
]
*Corollaire (5.1), there exists a dense, Zariski open subset
U\subseteq\mathcal{B}
such that
p^{-1}(U)\to U
and
q^{-1}(U)\to U
are locally topologically trivial fibrations (in the analytic topology). After replacing
\mathcal{B}
by
U
, we may therefore assume that
\varphi
induces a map
\varphi^{*}\colon R^{2}q_{*}\mathbb{Q}\to R^{2}p_{*}\mathbb{Q}
of local systems.

By the previous discussions, each
N^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}_{b})
is a fiber of the local system
\mathrm{coker}(\varphi^{*})
. As they also form the local system
\mathcal{G}\mathcal{N}^{1}(\mathcal{X}/B)
defined in
[
41
]
*Definition (12.2.4), we deduce from
[
41
]
*Proposition (12.2.5) that it has finite monodromy. We can then trivialize it by some dominant generically finite cover
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
to produce the desired isomorphism

N^{1}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\mathcal{Z}^{\prime})|_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{b}}\cong N^{1}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{b}).

Thus the lemma holds in this case.

In general, let
\pi\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
be a log resolution and let
\psi\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{Z}
the induced map. By shrinking
\mathcal{B}
, we may assume that
\mathcal{Y}
is smooth over
\mathcal{B}
. As
\mathcal{X}
has rational singularities, we have
R^{i}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}}=0
for all
i>0
and hence
R^{i}\psi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}}=R^{i}\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus from the smooth case treated above, we know that
N^{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}_{b})
and
N^{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{b}/\mathcal{X}_{b})
each form a local system with finite monodromy possibly after shrinking
\mathcal{B}
. Since
N^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}_{b})
is the kernel of the natural restriction map
N^{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}_{b})\to N^{1}(\mathcal{Y}_{b}/\mathcal{X}_{b})
, we deduce that
N^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}_{b})
also form a local system with finite monodromy and the lemma follows as before.
∎

The following provides the first step in the proof of Theorem
4.13
.

Corollary 4.17
.

Let
\varphi\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
be a Fano type fibration over a variety
\mathcal{B}
. Then there exists a generically finite dominant morphism
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
such that after base change
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=\mathcal{Z}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
, the natural restriction map

N^{1}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\mathcal{Z}^{\prime})\to N^{1}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{b})

is an isomorphism for every
b\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By
[
42
]
*Theorem 5.22 and Kawamata-Viehweg vanishing, we know that
\mathcal{X}
has rational singularities and
R^{1}\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}=R^{2}\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}=0
. The statement then follows directly from Lemma
4.16
.
∎

By essentially the same argument, we also get the following result.

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
\varphi\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
be a Fano type fibration over a variety
\mathcal{B}
. Then there exist a generically finite dominant morphism
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
and a small birational modification
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{Y}_{b}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial for every
b\in\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
\pi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}\to\mathcal{X}
be a log resolution. Note that for all
i>0
, we have
R^{i}\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}}=0
as
\mathcal{X}
has rational singularities by
[
42
]
*Theorem 5.22, and
R^{i}\varphi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}=0
by Kawamata-Viehweg vanishing, hence
R^{i}(\varphi\circ\pi)_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}}=0
for all
i>0
as well. Thus by Lemmas
4.14
and
4.16
, possibly after a generically finite base change, we may assume that
\mathcal{B}
is smooth,
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}
is smooth over
\mathcal{B}
, and there exist finitely many line bundles
\mathcal{L}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{L}_{r}
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}
whose restriction to the fibers
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{b}
generate
N^{1}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}_{b})
for all
b\in\mathcal{B}
. It follows that the class group
\mathrm{Cl}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
is generated by
\varphi^{*}\mathrm{Pic}(\mathcal{Z}_{b})
and
\pi_{*}\mathcal{L}_{i,b}
(
i=1,\dots,r
). As
\mathcal{X}
is of Fano type over
\mathcal{Z}
, it has a small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modification
\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
by
[
8
, Corollary 1.4.3]
. By shrinking
\mathcal{B}
again, we may assume that the induced map
\mathcal{Y}_{b}\to\mathcal{X}_{b}
is small and
\mathrm{Cl}(\mathcal{Y}_{b})\cong\mathrm{Cl}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
is generated by
\mathrm{Pic}(\mathcal{Z}_{b})
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i,b}
where
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i}
is the pushforward of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
to
\mathcal{Y}
. Since
\mathcal{Y}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, every
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier. This implies that
\mathcal{Y}_{b}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial.
∎

The next step (Lemma
4.20
) in the proof of Theorem
4.13
is to show that the relative pseudo-effective cone in
N^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}_{b})_{\mathbb{R}}
is also locally constant. Before we prove this, we need an auxiliary result.

Lemma 4.19
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair over
T
. Assume that
\Delta
has rational coefficients,
\Delta\geq A\geq 0
for some ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
A
. Then there exist a log resolution
\pi\colon X^{\prime}\to X
, a projective compactification
X^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{X}
over
T
, and an SNC
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\overline{\Delta}
on
\overline{X}
such that
\overline{X}\setminus X^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Supp}(\overline{\Delta})
,
\overline{\Delta}\geq\bar{A}\geq 0
for some ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\bar{A}
,
(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta})
is klt, and

K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E

for some
\pi
-exceptional
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
E\geq 0
, where
\Delta^{\prime}=\overline{\Delta}|_{X^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

By assumption, we may write
\Delta=A+B
where
B\geq 0
. First consider the case when
(X,\Delta)
is log smooth. Then there exists a projective compactification
X\subseteq X_{1}
over
T
such that
A
extends to an ample divisor
A_{1}
on
X_{1}
. Choose some log resolution
\varphi\colon\overline{X}\to X_{1}
that is an isomorphism over
X
such that the exceptional locus supports a
\varphi
-ample divisor
-F
and
D=\overline{X}\setminus X
is simple normal crossing. Then
\varphi^{*}A_{1}-\varepsilon F
is ample when
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
(
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{Q}
). Let
\overline{B}
be the closure of
B
in
\overline{X}
. Passing to a higher model we may assume that
\overline{B}
has SNC support. By Bertini’s theorem, we may choose some
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
0\leq\overline{A}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}\varphi^{*}A_{1}-\varepsilon F
such that
(\overline{X},\overline{\Delta}:=\overline{A}+\overline{B}+\frac{1}{2}D)
is log smooth and klt. By construction,
\overline{\Delta}
supports the ample divisor
\overline{A}
and
\overline{\Delta}|_{X}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}A+B=\Delta
. Hence the lemma holds in this case (with
X^{\prime}=X
).

In general, write
\Delta=A+B
as before and take a log resolution
\pi\colon X^{\prime}\to X
of
(X,\Delta)
whose exceptional locus supports a
\pi
-ample divisor
-F
. By the negativity lemma we have
F\geq 0
. We may write

K_{X^{\prime}}+B^{\prime}=\pi^{*}(K_{X}+B)+E

for some
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
B^{\prime},E\geq 0
such that
B^{\prime}
and
E
have no common components, and
E
is
\varphi
-exceptional. Since
(X,B)
is klt, the coefficients of
B^{\prime}
are less than
1
, thus the log smooth pair
(X^{\prime},B^{\prime})
is also klt. Choose some
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
such that
\varphi^{*}A-\varepsilon F
is ample and
(X^{\prime},B^{\prime}+\varepsilon F)
remains klt. By Bertini’s theorem, we may also choose some
0\leq A^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}\pi^{*}A-\varepsilon F
such that
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}:=A^{\prime}+B^{\prime}+\varepsilon F)
is still log smooth and klt. By construction, we see that
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E
and
\Delta^{\prime}
supports the ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
A^{\prime}
. Thus we may replace
(X,\Delta)
by
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
and reduce to the log smooth case treated before.
∎

The following gives the second main ingredient in the proof of Theorem
4.13
.

Lemma 4.20
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{B}
be a dominant morphism of varieties and let
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
be a Fano type fibration. Then there exists a dense open subset
U\subseteq\mathcal{B}
such that for any line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathcal{X}
and any closed point
b\in U
, we have
\mathcal{L}_{b}
is pseudo-effective (resp. big) over
\mathcal{Z}_{b}
if and only if
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
is pseudo-effective (resp. big) over
\mathcal{Z}_{\eta}
, where
\eta
is the generic point of
\mathcal{B}
.

Proof.

The statement is local on
\mathcal{Z}
, thus we may assume that
\mathcal{Z}
is affine, in which case a divisor on
\mathcal{X}
is big (resp. ample) over
\mathcal{Z}
if and only if it is big (resp. ample) by itself. Since
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
is a Fano type fibration, by shrinking
\mathcal{Z}
again we may assume that there exists some
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\mathcal{D}\geq 0
on
\mathcal{X}
such that
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})
is klt,
K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}0
, and
\mathcal{D}\geq A\geq 0
for some ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
A
. Since a divisor
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathcal{X}
is big if and only if
\mathcal{L}-\varepsilon A
is pseudo-effective for some
\varepsilon>0
, the pseudo-effective version of the lemma implies the big version. Thus it suffices to consider the pseudo-effective case of the statement.

Since
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Z}
is a Fano type fibration, by applying
[
8
]
*Corollary 1.3.2 to a small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorialization of
\mathcal{X}
, we know that there are finitely many divisors
\mathcal{D}_{i}\geq 0
on
\mathcal{X}
that generate the (relative) pseudo-effective cone; in other words, for any
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathcal{X}
that is pseudo-effective over
\mathcal{Z}
(equivalently,
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
is pseudo-effective over
\mathcal{Z}_{\eta}
) we have
\mathcal{L}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Z}}\sum a_{i}\mathcal{D}_{i}
for some
a_{i}\geq 0
. By shrinking
\mathcal{B}
we may assume that
\mathcal{B}
is smooth and affine,
\mathcal{X}
is flat over
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{X}_{b}
is normal, and
\mathcal{D}_{i}
does not contain
\mathcal{X}_{b}
for all
i
and all
b\in\mathcal{B}
. In particular, the restriction
\mathcal{D}_{i,b}
is well-defined and we have
\mathcal{L}_{b}\sim_{\mathbb{R},\mathcal{Z}_{b}}\sum a_{i}\mathcal{D}_{i,b}
, hence
\mathcal{L}_{b}
is pseudo-effective over
\mathcal{Z}_{b}
whenever
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
is pseudo-effective over
\mathcal{Z}_{\eta}
.

It remains to show that the converse direction holds after possibly further shrinking
\mathcal{B}
. For this, we first note that since
\mathcal{Z}
is affine, we can factorize
\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{B}
into an open immersion
\mathcal{Z}\to\overline{\mathcal{Z}}
followed by a projective morphism
\overline{\mathcal{Z}}\to\mathcal{B}
such that there is an ample Cartier divisor
\mathcal{M}_{0}
on
\overline{\mathcal{Z}}
with
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{M}_{0})=\overline{\mathcal{Z}}\setminus\mathcal{Z}
. By Lemma
4.19
, there exist a log resolution
\pi\colon\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
, a projective compactification
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{X}}
over
\overline{\mathcal{Z}}
and an SNC
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
on
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
such that
\overline{\mathcal{X}}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathrm{Supp}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})
,
\overline{\mathcal{D}}=\overline{A}+\overline{B}
where
\overline{A}\geq 0
is an ample
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor and
\overline{B}\geq 0
,
(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\overline{\mathcal{D}})
is klt, and

K_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}\pi^{*}(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})+\mathcal{E}

for some
\pi
-exceptional
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\mathcal{E}\geq 0
, where
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\overline{\mathcal{D}}|_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
. Let
U\subseteq\mathcal{B}
be an affine open subset such that
(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathcal{D}_{b})
is klt (in particular, this implies that
\mathcal{X}_{b}\to\mathcal{Z}_{b}
is a Fano type fibration) for all
b\in U
, and
(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\overline{\mathcal{D}})
is log smooth over
U
. Let
\mathcal{M}
be the pullback of
\mathcal{M}_{0}
to
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
. By construction,
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
is projective over
\mathcal{B}
, the divisor
\mathcal{M}
is semiample over
\mathcal{B}
, and
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{M})=\overline{\mathcal{X}}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
.

Now let
\mathcal{L}
be a line bundle on
\mathcal{X}
such that
\mathcal{L}_{b}
is pseudo-effective over
\mathcal{Z}_{b}
. Then
H^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\ell\mathcal{L}_{b})\neq 0
for some positive integer
\ell
since
\mathcal{X}_{b}
is of Fano type over
\mathcal{Z}_{b}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{b}
is affine. As
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
is smooth, we may extend
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}:=\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}
to a line bundle
\overline{\mathcal{L}}
on
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
. Note that for all sufficiently divisible positive integer
m
we have
