m(K_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})+\ell\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\sim m(\pi^{*}(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})+\mathcal{E})+\ell\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}\sim m\mathcal{E}+\ell\pi^{*}\mathcal{L}.

As
H^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\ell\mathcal{L}_{b})\neq 0
and
\mathcal{E}\geq 0
, this implies that

H^{0}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{b},m(K_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{b}}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{b})+\ell\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{b})\neq 0.

Since
\mathcal{E}
is
\pi
-exceptional, it also implies that
\mathcal{L}
is pseudo-effective as long as
m(K_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})+\ell\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is pseudo-effective for some positive integers
\ell,m
. Choose some sufficiently large and divisible integer
m
(which depends on
\ell
) such that
m\overline{A}
and
m\overline{B}
are Cartier, and
m\overline{A}+\ell\overline{\mathcal{L}}
is ample. Since
H^{0}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{b},m(K_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{b}}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{b})+\ell\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{b})\neq 0
and
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{M})=\overline{\mathcal{X}}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, we may take a sufficiently large integer
r
(depending on
\ell
and
m
) such that

(4.5)

H^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{b},m(K_{\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{b}}+\overline{\mathcal{D}}_{b})+\ell\overline{\mathcal{L}}_{b}+r\mathcal{M}_{b})\neq 0.

By construction, we have

m(K_{\overline{\mathcal{X}}}+\overline{\mathcal{D}})+\ell\overline{\mathcal{L}}+r\mathcal{M}=m(K_{\overline{\mathcal{X}}}+\overline{B})+(m\overline{A}+\ell\overline{\mathcal{L}}+r\mathcal{M}).

Note that
(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\overline{B})
is log smooth over
\mathcal{B}
, and
m\overline{A}+\overline{\mathcal{L}}+r\mathcal{M}
is ample, thus by (
4.5
) and
[
24
]
*Theorem 1.8, we deduce that

H^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{X}},m(K_{\overline{\mathcal{X}}}+\overline{\mathcal{D}})+\ell\overline{\mathcal{L}}+r\mathcal{M})\neq 0.

By restriction to
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
we also get a section of
m(K_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime})+\ell\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
. From the previous discussion, we deduce that
\mathcal{L}
is pseudo-effective and this concludes the proof.
∎

We now return to the proof of Theorem
4.13
. We first consider a special case.

Lemma 4.21
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a bounded set of Fano type fibrations. Then there exists a bounded set
\mathcal{T}
of Fano type fibrations such that for any
(X,\Delta)\to Z
in
\mathcal{S}
and any birational contraction
f\colon X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
over
Z
, we have
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}=f_{*}\Delta)\to Z
belongs to
\mathcal{T}
.

Proof.

We may assume that
\mathcal{S}
is given by the fibers of a Fano type fibration
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{Z}
over a finite type base
\mathcal{B}
. By induction on
\dim\mathcal{B}
, it suffices to prove the statement after some generically finite dominant base change
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
. Thus we may shrink
\mathcal{B}
freely as needed. In particular we assume that
\mathcal{B}
is smooth and connected throughout the proof.

By Lemma
4.18
, possibly after a generically finite dominant base change and replacing
\mathcal{X}
by a small birational modification, we may assume that
\mathcal{X}_{b}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial for all
b\in\mathcal{B}
. Note that a small birational modification does not change the set of birational contractions. By Corollary
4.17
, possibly after another generically finite dominant base change, we may also assume that
N^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b}/\mathcal{Z}_{b})
form a trivial local system. By
[
8
]
*Corollary 1.3.1, there are only finitely many birational contractions
f_{i}\colon\mathcal{X}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{i}
(
i=1,\dots,m
) over
\mathcal{Z}
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{i}
be the sum of all the exceptional divisors of
f_{i}
. By shrinking the base
\mathcal{B}
again, we can assume that the following holds simultaneously for all
i
and all
b\in\mathcal{B}
:

•

\mathcal{X}_{i,b}
is normal,

•

\mathcal{E}_{i,b}\neq 0
whenever
\mathcal{E}_{i}\neq 0
,

•

the map
f_{i}
is defined at the generic point of
\mathcal{X}_{b}
,

•

the induced map
f_{i,b}\colon\mathcal{X}_{b}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{i,b}
is a birational contraction,

•

\mathcal{E}_{i,b}
is contained in the exceptional locus of
f_{i,b}
, and

•

f_{i,b}
is small whenever
f_{i}
is small.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{b}\dashrightarrow Y
be a birational contraction over
\mathcal{Z}_{b}
. Let
H
be a very ample Cartier divisor on
Y
and let
\mathcal{L}_{b}
be its birational pullback to
\mathcal{X}_{b}
. Then
\mathcal{L}_{b}
is big and movable (i.e. the base locus of
|\mathcal{L}_{b}|
has codimension at least
2
in
\mathcal{X}_{b}
). It is also
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier since
\mathcal{X}_{b}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial. By Lemma
4.20
, possibly after replacing
\mathcal{L}_{b}
by a multiple we may extend it to a big line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathcal{X}
. The ample model of
\mathcal{L}
over
\mathcal{Z}
necessarily coincides with one of the
f_{i}\colon\mathcal{X}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{i}
. We claim that
Y\cong\mathcal{X}_{i,b}
.

To see this, first note that the
\mathcal{L}
-MMP over
\mathcal{Z}
coincides with some
f_{j}\colon\mathcal{X}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{j}
. We first show that
f_{j}
is small. Let
\mathcal{L}_{j}=f_{j*}\mathcal{L}
which is nef over
\mathcal{Z}
. By the negativity lemma (see e.g.
[
42
]
*Lemma 3.38), we have
\mathcal{L}=f_{j}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{j}+\mathcal{E}
for some divisor
\mathcal{E}\geq 0
whose support contains every exceptional divisor of
f_{j}
. If
f_{j}
is not small, then
\mathcal{E}\neq 0
. Restricting to the fiber we see that
\mathcal{L}_{b}=f_{j,b}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{j,b}+\mathcal{E}_{b}
and hence the base locus of
\mathcal{L}_{b}
contains the divisor
\mathcal{E}_{b}\neq 0
, a contraction as
\mathcal{L}_{b}
is movable. Thus
f_{j}
is small and so is
f_{j,b}
(by the last bullet point above). The ample model
\mathcal{X}_{i}
(resp.
Y
) of
\mathcal{L}
(resp.
\mathcal{L}_{b}
) is therefore the same as the ample model of
\mathcal{L}_{j}
(resp.
\mathcal{L}_{j,b}
) over
\mathcal{Z}
(resp.
\mathcal{Z}_{b}
). Since
\mathcal{L}_{j}
is nef over
\mathcal{Z}
, its ample model is a morphism
\mathcal{X}_{j}\to\mathcal{X}_{i}
; combined with the fact that
\mathcal{X}_{i,b}
is normal, we see that
\mathcal{X}_{i,b}
is also the ample model of
\mathcal{L}_{j,b}
. This proves that
Y\cong\mathcal{X}_{i,b}
. It follows that every birational contraction of
(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathcal{D}_{b})
over
\mathcal{Z}_{b}
is a fiber of the finitely many families
(\mathcal{X}_{i},\mathcal{D}_{i}=f_{i*}\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{Z}
over
\mathcal{B}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.13
.

By Lemma
2.33
, we may assume that
\mathcal{S}
is given by the fibers of an
\mathbb{R}
-Gorenstein family
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{Z}
of log Fano fibrations over a finite type base
\mathcal{B}
. By the proof of
[
27
]
*Proposition 2.5 (or by Lemma
4.18
and
loc. cit.
), possibly after a base change via some surjective morphism
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
, there exists a crepant birational morphism
(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{E})\to(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})
whose restriction to the fiber over
b\in\mathcal{B}
extracts every divisor of non-positive discrepancy over
(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathcal{D}_{b})
. Note that
(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{E})
is also of Fano type over
\mathcal{Z}
by Lemma
2.29
, and every birational model
f\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{b}
such that
f^{*}(K_{\mathcal{X}_{b}}+\mathcal{D}_{b})=K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}
for some
\Delta^{\prime}\geq 0
can be obtained by a birational contraction
\mathcal{Y}_{b}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
. The theorem thus follows from Lemma
4.21
.
∎

4.5.
Special boundedness

To prove Theorems
4.2
and
4.3
, we still need to upgrade Theorem
4.13
to allow specially bounded input. The precise statement is the following.

Theorem 4.22
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a specially bounded set of log Fano fibration germs. Let
\mathcal{T}
be the set of Fano type fibration germs
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to Z\ni z
for which there exist some
(X,\Delta)\to Z\ni z
in
\mathcal{S}
and a birational map
f\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X
over
Z
such that
f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)\geq K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}
. Then
\mathcal{T}
is log specially bounded.

To prove this, we need a few auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma 4.23
.

Let
(X,G+D)
be a log canonical pair where
G
is a normal Weil divisor and
D\geq 0
, and let
X\to Z
a projective morphism such that
-(K_{X}+G+D)
is semiample over
Z
. Let
f\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X
be a birational map over
Z
where
X^{\prime}
is projective over
Z
. Let
G^{\prime}
be the strict transform of
G
on
X^{\prime}
and let
D^{\prime}
be an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X^{\prime}
such that

f^{*}(K_{X}+G+D)\geq K_{X^{\prime}}+G^{\prime}+D^{\prime}.

Assume that
G^{\prime}
is normal and Cartier on
X^{\prime}
. Then for the induced birational map
f_{G}\colon G^{\prime}\dashrightarrow G
, we have

f_{G}^{*}(K_{G}+\mathrm{Diff}_{G}(D))\geq K_{G^{\prime}}+D^{\prime}|_{G^{\prime}}.

Proof.

By enlarging
D^{\prime}
we may assume that
f^{*}(K_{X}+G+D)=K_{X^{\prime}}+G^{\prime}+D^{\prime}
. The statement is local on
Z
, hence we may also assume that
-(K_{X}+G+D)
is semiample. By Bertini’s theorem, we can choose some
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
0\leq H\sim_{\mathbb{R}}-(K_{X}+G+D)
such that
H
(resp.
H|_{G}
) does not contain any exceptional divisor of
f
(resp.
f_{G}
) in its support. In particular, we may assume that
f
is an isomorphism at every generic point of
\mathrm{Supp}(H)\cap G
, hence
f^{*}H=H^{\prime}
and
f_{G}^{*}(H|_{G})\leq H^{\prime}|_{G^{\prime}}
, where
H^{\prime}
is the strict transform of
H
on
X^{\prime}
. As
K_{X}+G+D+H\sim_{\mathbb{R}}0
, we also have
f^{*}(K_{X}+G+D+H)=K_{X^{\prime}}+G^{\prime}+D^{\prime}+H^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}0
and this is a crepant pullback. By adjunction, we deduce that

f_{G}^{*}(K_{G}+\mathrm{Diff}_{G}(D+H))=K_{G^{\prime}}+\mathrm{Diff}_{G^{\prime}}(D^{\prime}+H^{\prime}).

Note that
\mathrm{Diff}_{G}(D+H)=\mathrm{Diff}_{G}(D)+H|_{G}
since
H
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier and
\mathrm{Diff}_{G^{\prime}}(D^{\prime}+H^{\prime})=(D^{\prime}+H^{\prime})|_{G^{\prime}}
since
G^{\prime}
is Cartier. Thus

f_{G}^{*}(K_{G}+\mathrm{Diff}_{G}(D))=K_{G^{\prime}}+(D^{\prime}+H^{\prime})|_{G^{\prime}}-f_{G}^{*}(H|_{G})\geq K_{G^{\prime}}+D^{\prime}|_{S^{\prime}}

as desired.
∎

Lemma 4.24
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{Z}
be a special test configuration of a log Fano fibration
(X,\Delta)\to Z
. Let
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
be a projective pair over
Z
and
f\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X
a birational map over
Z
such that

(4.6)

f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)\geq K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}.

Then there exist a Fano type test configuration
(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\to\mathcal{Z}
of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to Z
and a birational map
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}
over
\mathcal{Z}
extending
f
, such that
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{0}
is the strict transform of
\mathcal{X}_{0}
and the induced birational map
f_{0}\colon\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{0}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{0}
over
\mathcal{Z}_{0}
satisfies

(4.7)

f_{0}^{*}(K_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}+\mathcal{D}_{0})\geq K_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{0}}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{0}.

Proof.

Let
E_{1},\dots,E_{r}
be the prime divisors on
X^{\prime}
that are contracted by
f
. Through the isomorphism
\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}_{0}\cong X\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
, each
E_{i}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
is identified with a divisor
\mathcal{E}_{i}
over
\mathcal{X}
. By assumption,
a(E_{i},X,\Delta)\leq 0
, thus
a(\mathcal{E}_{i},\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\leq 0
. By
[
8
]
*Corollary 1.4.3, we know that there exists a projective birational morphism
h\colon\mathcal{Y}\to\mathcal{X}
such that
\mathcal{Y}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial and the exceptional divisors are exactly given by
\mathcal{E}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{E}_{r}
. Since
\mathcal{E}_{i}
is invariant under the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action, we also know that
h
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant. By construction,
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
is irreducible (as all the
h
-exceptional divisors dominate
\mathbb{A}^{1}
), and the induced birational map
\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Y}_{0}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
is a birational contraction.

Let
L
be an ample line bundle on
X^{\prime}
and choose some Weil divisor
\mathcal{L}
on
\mathcal{Y}
whose restriction to
\mathcal{Y}\setminus\mathcal{Y}_{0}
is the birational pullback of
L\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
. Since
\mathcal{Y}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial, we know that
\mathcal{L}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier. Note that
\mathcal{Y}
is of Fano type over
\mathcal{Z}
by Lemma
2.29
, hence the ample model
g\colon\mathcal{Y}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{L}
over
\mathcal{Z}
exists and is automatically
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant. By construction we have
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{0}\cong X^{\prime}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
and the induced map
\widetilde{f}\colon\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}
extends
f
. Note that as
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
is irreducible and semiample, it is not in the base locus of
\mathcal{L}
, thus
g
is an isomorphism at the generic point of
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
. It follows that
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{0}
is the strict transform of
\mathcal{Y}_{0}
(in particular, it is irreducible), and we have an induced birational map
f_{0}\colon\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{0}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{0}
.

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
be the closure of
\Delta^{\prime}\times(\mathbb{A}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
in
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
. By (
4.6
), we have

\widetilde{f}^{*}(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{D})\geq K_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime}.

As
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{Z}
is a special test configuration, we see that
(\mathcal{X}^{\prime},\mathcal{D}^{\prime})\to\mathcal{Z}
is a Fano type test configuration of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to Z
by Lemma
2.35
. Note that we also have
\widetilde{f}^{*}\mathcal{X}_{0}=\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{0}
, hence
\widetilde{f}^{*}(K_{\mathcal{X}}+\mathcal{X}_{0}+\mathcal{D})\geq K_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{X}^{\prime}_{0}+\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
. Thus we obtain (
4.7
) by Lemma
4.23
.
∎

We are ready to prove Theorem
4.22
.

Proof of Theorem
4.22
.

By assumption, there exists a bounded set
\mathcal{S}_{0}
of log Fano fibrations such that any fibration germ
(X,\Delta)\to Z\ni z
in
\mathcal{S}
specially degenerates to some
(X_{0},\Delta_{0})\to Z_{0}\ni z_{0}
in
\mathcal{S}_{0}
. If
f\colon X^{\prime}\dashrightarrow X
is a birational map over
Z
such that
f^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)\geq K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}
for some
\Delta^{\prime}\geq 0
, then by Lemma
4.24
we get an induced Fano type test configuration of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to Z\ni z
whose central fiber is of the form
(X^{\prime}_{0},\Delta^{\prime}_{0})\to Z_{0}\ni z_{0}
for some birational model
f_{0}\colon X^{\prime}_{0}\dashrightarrow X_{0}
such that
f_{0}^{*}(K_{X_{0}}+\Delta_{0})\geq K_{X^{\prime}_{0}}+\Delta^{\prime}_{0}
. Write
f_{0}^{*}(K_{X_{0}}+\Delta_{0})=K_{X^{\prime}_{0}}+D^{\prime}_{0}
. Then
D^{\prime}_{0}\geq\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\geq 0
and
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime}_{0})\subseteq\mathrm{Supp}(D^{\prime}_{0})
. By Theorem
4.13
, the set of such fibrations
(X^{\prime}_{0},D^{\prime}_{0})\to Z_{0}
is bounded, hence the set of
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to Z\ni z
is log specially bounded.
∎

We now have all the ingredients for the proof of Theorems
4.2
and
4.3
.

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

Let
X\to Z\ni z
be a Fano type fibration germ such that
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)\geq\varepsilon
for some
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
0\leq\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}-K_{X}
. By
[
8
]
*Corollary 1.4.3, a small
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial modification
X^{\prime\prime}\to X
exists. By Lemma
2.29
and
[
8
]
*Corollary 1.3.2, the ample model
X^{\prime\prime}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
of
-K_{X^{\prime\prime}}
over
Z
also exists. Note that
X^{\prime}\to Z
is a log Fano fibration by construction. Let
h\colon X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
be the induced birational contraction and let
\Delta^{\prime}
be the strict transform of
\Delta
on
X^{\prime}
. Since
K_{X}+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}0
, we have
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}0
as well. In particular, the birational map
(X,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is crepant. By Lemma
3.11
, we have
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})\geq\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X}(\Delta)\geq\varepsilon
, hence by Theorem
4.5
, we know that
X^{\prime}\to Z\ni z
belongs to a specially bounded set of log Fano fibration germs.

By the negativity lemma, we have
-K_{X}\geq h^{*}(-K_{X^{\prime}})
, or
h^{*}K_{X^{\prime}}\geq K_{X}
. By Theorem
4.22
, we see that the Fano type fibration germ
X\to Z\ni z
belongs to a log specially bounded set. By definition, this implies that the set
\mathcal{S}
of such fibrations is bounded up to Fano type degenerations.
∎

As explained in Section
4.1
, this also completes the proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Proof of Theorem
4.3
.

By perturbing
\delta
, we may assume that
\delta\in\mathbb{Q}
. Let
(X,\Delta)\to Z\ni z
be a log Calabi-Yau fibration germ in
\mathcal{S}
. Let
\Delta_{0}=\frac{\delta}{2}\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
. Since
X
is of Fano type and
(X,\Delta)
is log Calabi-Yau over
Z
, it is not hard to see that their convex combination
(X,\frac{1}{2}\Delta)
is of Fano type over
Z
. Therefore, since
\Delta_{0}\leq\frac{1}{2}\Delta
, we see that
(X,\Delta_{0})
is of Fano type over
Z
as well. Similar to the above proof of Theorem
4.2
, let
h\colon X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
be the ample model of
-(K_{X}+\Delta_{0})
which exists by
[
8
]
*Corollary 1.3.2. Let
\Delta^{\prime}=h_{*}\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}_{0}=h_{*}\Delta_{0}
. We have
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X^{\prime}}(\Delta^{\prime})\geq\varepsilon
by Lemma
3.11
, and
h^{*}(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta_{0}^{\prime})\geq K_{X}+\Delta_{0}
by the negativity lemma. By Lemma
3.14
, we also have
\widehat{\rm Vol}_{X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}_{0}}(\Delta^{\prime}-\Delta^{\prime}_{0})\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{2^{n}}
since
\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\leq\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{\prime}
by construction. Note that
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}0
and thus
\Delta^{\prime}-\Delta^{\prime}_{0}\sim_{\mathbb{R},Z}-(K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}_{0})
. By Theorem
4.5
, it follows that the log Fano fibration germ
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}_{0})\to Z\ni z
belongs to a specially bounded set. By Theorem
4.22
, the Fano type fibrations
(X,\Delta_{0})\to Z\ni z
belong to a log specially bounded set. As
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta_{0})=\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
, the set
\mathcal{S}
is also log specially bounded.
∎

5.
Termination in low dimension

In this section, we apply the results obtained in Section
3
to prove some effective termination result for general type MMPs in dimensions at most
5
. In particular, we prove Theorem
1.3
.

5.1.
Termination

We first prove a non-effective version of termination in dimensions four and five.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair. Assume that
\Delta
is big, and either

(1)

\dim X=4
, or

(2)

\dim X=5
and
K_{X}+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R}}D\geq 0
for some
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
D
.

Then any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP terminates.

Before proving this result, we give an immediate corollary.

Corollary 5.2
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair of dimension at most
5
and
\pi:X\rightarrow T
a projective morphism. Assume that at least one of the following holds.

(1)

K_{X}+\Delta
is big over
T
.

(2)

K_{X}+\Delta
is pseudo-effective over
T
and
\Delta
is big over
T
.

(3)

\dim X=4
and
\Delta
is big over
T
.

Then any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
T
terminates.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.18
, we may assume that
\Delta
is big in all cases. Then (3) follows from Theorem
5.1
(1), and (1) follows from (2), so we only need to prove (2) when
\dim X=5
.

By
[
8
]
*Theorem D, there exists an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
D\geq 0
on
X
, such that
K_{X}+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R},T}D
, or equivalently, there exists an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
G_{T}
on
T
such that
K_{X}+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R}}D+\pi^{*}G_{T}
. Let
H_{T}\geq 0
be an ample divisor on
T
such that
H_{T}+G_{T}\geq 0
. By Bertini’s theorem, there exists an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
0\leq\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta+\pi^{*}H_{T}
on
X
such that
(X,\Delta^{\prime})
is klt. Then
K_{X}+\Delta^{\prime}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}K_{X}+\Delta+\pi^{*}H_{T}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}D+\pi^{*}(G_{T}+H_{T})\geq 0
and any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
T
is a also a
(K_{X}+\Delta^{\prime})
-MMP, thus we conclude (2) by Theorem
5.1
(2).
∎

In the rest of this subsection we prove Theorem
5.1
. We first consider the 4-fold case. In view of Theorem
3.2
, the key is to prove the following statement.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair of dimension at most
4
and let
S\subseteq\mathbb{R}
be a discrete set. Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\Delta)=:(X_{0},\Delta_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{1},\Delta_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{i},\Delta_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}

be a face-contracting
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP (Definition
2.14
) such that
a(E,X_{i},\Delta_{i})\in S
for all
i
and all prime divisors
E
over
X
. Then this face-contracting MMP terminates.

Proof.

Since
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
is klt, by
[
42
, Proposition 2.36]
there are only finitely many prime divisors
E
over
X
with
a(E,X_{i},\Delta_{i})\leq 0
. The number
l_{i}
of such divisors is non-increasing in the MMP, thus after possibly truncating the MMP we may assume that this number stabilizes, i.e.
l_{0}=l_{1}=\cdots=l
for some
l\in\mathbb{N}
. Let

E_{1},\dots,E_{l}
be the corresponding prime divisors over
X
with
a(E_{j},X_{i},\Delta_{i})\leq 0
for all
i
and
j
.

Since
S
is discrete,
S\cap(-1,0]
is a finite set. Thus as
a(E_{j},X_{i},\Delta_{i})\in S\cap(-1,0]
by assumption and
a(E_{j},X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})\geq a(E_{j},X_{i},\Delta_{i})
for all
i
and
j
, after another truncation of the MMP we may assume that

a(E_{j},X_{0},\Delta_{0})=a(E_{j},X_{1},\Delta_{1})=\cdots=a(E_{j},X_{i},\Delta_{i})=\cdots

for all
j=1,\cdots,l
. Let
\pi:(Y,D)\rightarrow(X,\Delta)
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization of
(X,\Delta)
. By Lemma
2.17
, there exists a sequence of birational maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y,D)=:(Y_{0},D_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y_{1},D_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y_{i},D_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}}

such that each
(Y_{i},D_{i})
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization of
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
and each
\psi_{i}
is composed of a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y_{i}}+D_{i})
-MMP. As
\dim X\leq 4
, this sequence terminates by Lemma
2.20
, hence the original face-contracting
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP also terminates.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.1
when
\dim X=4
.

By Theorem
3.1
, the Cartier index of
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisors is uniformly bounded in the MMP, thus by Lemma
2.2
, the discrepancy of primes divisors over
X
takes values in some discrete set in the MMP. We then conclude by Lemma
5.3
.
∎

We move on to the
5
-fold case of Theorem
5.1
. A key observation is that since the Cariter index of
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisors are bounded from above by Theorem
3.2
, in Birkar’s inductive approach to termination
[
9
]
, we only need termination of flips in lower dimensions when the index is bounded.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
(Y,D)
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair of dimension
5
. Let

(5.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y,D)=:(Y_{0},D_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y_{1},D_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y_{i},D_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}}

be a
(K_{Y}+D)
-MMP. Assume that there exists some integer
r\in\mathbb{N}
such that the Cartier index of every
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor on
Y_{i}
is at most
r
for every
i
. Then
\psi_{i}
is an isomorphism near
\lfloor D_{i}\rfloor
for any
i\gg 0
.

Remark 5.5
.

As will be clear from the proof, if Lemma
5.3
holds in dimension
n-1
, then the above lemma holds in dimension
n
.

Proof.

We only outline the main steps since this essentially follows from the same proof of the special termination theorem, see e.g.
[
23
]
*Proof of Theorem 4.2.1. First note that by the negativity lemma as in Step 1 of
loc. cit.
, after truncating the MMP we may assume that it is an isomorphism at the generic point of every log canonical center of
(Y,D)
. Let
W\subseteq\mathrm{Supp}\lfloor D\rfloor
be a log canonical center of
(Y,D)
. Let
W_{i}
be its strict transform on
Y_{i}
and define
B_{i}
by adjunction
(K_{Y_{i}}+D_{i})|_{W_{i}}=K_{W_{i}}+B_{i}
. We next prove by induction on
\dim W
that the induced birational map
\varphi_{i}\colon W_{i}\dashrightarrow W_{i+1}
induces an isomorphism of pairs
(W_{i},B_{i})\cong(W_{i+1},B_{i+1})
when
i\gg 0
. The base case
\dim W=0
is clear, hence we may assume that
\dim W\geq 1
. Let
B=B_{0}
. By Lemma
2.19
and the induction hypothesis, we see that the birational map
W_{i}\dashrightarrow W_{i+1}
is an isomorphism near
\lfloor B_{i}\rfloor
for any
i\gg 0
. By
[
23
]
*Proposition 4.2.14 and Lemma 4.2.15, after another truncation of the MMP we may assume the induced sequence of birational maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(W_{0},B_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(W_{1},B_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(W_{i},B_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}

is a face-contracting
(K_{W}+B)
-MMP. Let
U=W\setminus\lfloor B\rfloor
and
B_{U}=B|_{U}
. Then we also get a sequence of
(K_{U}+B_{U})
-MMP

(5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{0},B_{U_{0}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{1},B_{U_{1}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{i},B_{U_{i}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}

where
U_{i}=W_{i}\setminus\lfloor B_{i}\rfloor
and
B_{U_{i}}=B_{i}|_{U_{i}}
. Note that
(U,B_{U})
is klt and
\dim U\leq 4
. Since the index of every
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor on
Y_{i}
is at most
r
for every
i
, by Lemma
2.2
and Lemma
5.3
we deduce that (
5.2
) becomes an isomorphism when
i\gg 0
. This completes the induction. Since every component of
\lfloor D_{i}\rfloor
is a log canonical center of
(Y_{i},D_{i})
, the statement now follows from another application of Lemma
2.19
.
∎

To apply Lemma
5.4
, we need to verify the uniform boundedness of the Cartier index when we lift an MMP to some dlt modification.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
(X,B)
be a log canonical pair and let
\pi\colon(Y,D)\to(X,B)
be a crepant birational morphism such that
\mathrm{mult}_{E}D>0
for all
\pi
-exceptional divisor
E
. Assume that there exists a big
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\Delta\geq 0
on
X
such that
(X,\Delta)
is klt and
K_{X}+B\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\mu(K_{X}+\Delta)
for some
\mu>0
. Then there exists a positive integer
r
, such that for any MMP type contraction
\psi\colon(Y,D)\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime},D^{\prime})
with
D^{\prime}=\psi_{*}D
, the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor on
Y^{\prime}
is at most
r
.

Proof.

Since
\Delta
is big, after possibly replacing
\Delta
by some other
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
0\leq\Delta_{0}\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\Delta
we may assume by Lemma
2.18
that
\Delta\geq A
where
A\geq 0
is an ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor. Since
(X,\Delta)
is klt and
\mathrm{mult}_{E}D>0
for any
\pi
-exceptional prime divisor
E
, we can choose some
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
such that

•

the pair
(X,\Delta^{\prime}:=(1-\varepsilon)B+\varepsilon\Delta)
is klt, and

•

\mathrm{mult}_{E}G>0
for any
\pi
-exceptional prime divisor
E
, where the
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
G
is defined by crepant pullback:
K_{Y}+G=\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta^{\prime})
.

In particular,
\mathrm{Supp}(\pi^{*}A)\subseteq\mathrm{Supp}(G)
and hence
G
is big. We have

K_{Y}+G=(1-\varepsilon)\pi^{*}(K_{X}+B)+\varepsilon\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)\sim_{\mathbb{R}}(1-\varepsilon+\varepsilon\mu^{-1})(K_{Y}+D).

Thus as
\psi\colon(Y,D)\dashrightarrow(Y^{\prime},D^{\prime})
is an MMP type contraction, by Lemma
2.13
it also induces an MMP type contraction
(Y,G)\dashrightarrow(Y,G^{\prime})
with
G^{\prime}=\psi_{*}G
. We then conclude by Theorem
3.2
.
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial klt pair of dimension
5
and let
B
be an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
X
such that
(X,B)
is log canonical. Assume that
\Delta
is big and
K_{X}+B\sim_{\mathbb{R}}\mu(K_{X}+\Delta)
for some
\mu>0
. Let

(5.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,B)=:(X_{0},B_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{1},B_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{i},B_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}

be a
(K_{X}+B)
-MMP. Then
\varphi_{i}
is an isomorphism near
\mathrm{Nklt}(X_{i},B_{i})
for any
i\gg 0
.

Proof.

By
[
47
, Corollary 1.36]
, there exists a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial dlt pair
(Y,D)
and a crepant birational morphism
\pi\colon(Y,D)\to(X,B)
such that every
\pi
-exceptional divisor has coefficient
1
in
D
. By Lemma
2.15
, we can lift (
5.3
) to a sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y,D)=:(Y_{0},D_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y_{1},D_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y_{i},D_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}}

such that each
\psi_{i}
is a sequence of steps of a
(K_{Y_{i}}+D_{i})
-MMP, and there are crepant birational morphisms
\pi_{i}:(Y_{i},D_{i})\to(X_{i},B_{i})
. We claim that
\mathrm{Ex}(\pi_{i})\subseteq\lfloor D_{i}\rfloor
for all
i
. In fact, since
X_{i}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial,
\mathrm{Ex}(\pi_{i})
is of pure codimension one in
Y_{i}
by
[
42
, Corollary 2.63]
. In particular, the claim is clear when
i=0
, so we may assume that
i\geq 1
. For any irreducible component
E
of
\mathrm{Ex}(\pi_{i})
, we have

a(E,X_{i},\Delta_{i})=-\mathrm{mult}_{E}D_{i}=-\mathrm{mult}_{E}D_{i-1}=a(E,X_{i-1},\Delta_{i-1}),

so
E
cannot be the strict transform of some
\varphi_{i-1}
-exceptional divisor (otherwise we get a contradiction to the negativity lemma). It follows that the strict transform of
E
on
Y_{i-1}
is contained in
\mathrm{Ex}(\pi_{i-1})
, hence
\mathrm{Ex}(\pi_{i})=(\psi_{i-1})_{*}\mathrm{Ex}(\pi_{i-1})
, and the claim follows by induction on
i
.

By Lemma
5.6
, there exists some positive integer
r
such that the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor on
Y_{i}
is at most
r
for all
i
. Thus by Lemma
5.4
, we see that
\psi_{i}
is an isomorphism near
\lfloor D_{i}\rfloor
for any
i\gg 0
. Since
(Y_{i},D_{i})
is dlt and
\pi_{i}
is crepant, combined with the previous claim
\mathrm{Ex}(\pi_{i})\subseteq\lfloor D_{i}\rfloor
we see that
\lfloor D_{i}\rfloor=\pi_{i}^{-1}(\mathrm{Nklt}(X_{i},B_{i}))
. Therefore,
\varphi_{i}
is also an isomorphism near
\mathrm{Nklt}(X_{i},B_{i})
for any
i\gg 0
; otherwise, by the negativity lemma, for some
i\gg 0
we can find a prime divisor
E
over
X
whose center is contained in
\mathrm{Nklt}(X_{i},B_{i})
such that

a(E,Y_{i},D_{i})=a(E,X_{i},B_{i})<a(E,X_{i+1},B_{i+1})=a(E,Y_{i+1},D_{i+1}),

which is a contradiction as the center of
E
on
Y_{i}
is contained in
\lfloor D_{i}\rfloor
and
\psi_{i}
is an isomorphism around
\lfloor D_{i}\rfloor
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.1
when
\dim X=5
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\Delta)=:(X_{0},\Delta_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{1},\Delta_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{i},\Delta_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}

be a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP. Let
D_{i}
be the strict transform of
D
on
X_{i}
, and let
t_{0}=\mathrm{lct}(X,\Delta;D)
. Then
(X,\Delta+t_{0}D)
is log canonical and
K_{X}+\Delta+t_{0}D\sim_{\mathbb{R}}(1+t_{0})(K_{X}+\Delta)
. Since
K_{X}+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{R}}D
, the MMP is an isomorphism away from
\mathrm{Supp}(D_{i})
and any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP is also a
(K_{X}+\Delta+t_{0}D)
-MMP. By Lemma
5.7
, after finitely many steps, the MMP is an isomorphism near
\mathrm{Nklt}(X_{i},\Delta_{i}+t_{0}D_{i})
. In other words, after truncation it induces a sequence of
(K_{U}+\Delta_{U})
-MMP where
U=X\setminus\mathrm{Nklt}(X,\Delta+t_{0}D)
and
\Delta_{U}=\Delta|_{U}
. Now
(U,\Delta_{U}+t_{0}D_{U})
is klt (where
D_{U}=D|_{U}
) and hence
t_{1}:=\mathrm{lct}(U,\Delta_{U};D_{U})>t_{0}
. Replacing
(X,\Delta)
,
D
with
(U,\Delta_{U})
,
D_{U}
and repeat the same argument, we see that the MMP is again an isomorphism near
\mathrm{Nklt}(X_{i},\Delta_{i}+t_{1}D_{i})
after finitely many steps, and thus we can inductively construct, if the MMP does not terminate, a strictly increasing sequence
t_{0}<t_{1}<\dots
of log canonical thresholds, where each
t_{i}
has the form
\mathrm{lct}_{x_{j}}(X_{j},\Delta_{j};D_{j})
for some
j\in\mathbb{N}
and some point
x_{j}\in X_{j}
. This contradicts the ACC of log canonical thresholds
[
25
]
*Theorem 1.1. It follows that the MMP must terminate.
∎

5.2.
Effective termination

We next upgrade the termination result from the previous subsection to an effective one and give some further applications.

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair of dimension
\leq 5
and
X\to T
a projective morphism. Assume that at least one of the following holds.

(1)

K_{X}+\Delta
is big over
T
,

(2)

K_{X}+\Delta
is pseudo-effective over
T
, and
\Delta
is big over
T
,

(3)

\dim X\leq 4
and
\Delta
is big over
T
.

Then there are finitely many birational maps
\phi_{i}:X\dashrightarrow Y_{i}
over
T
,
1\leq i\leq m
, such that for any sequences of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP
\phi:X\dashrightarrow Y
over
T
, we have
\phi=\phi_{i}
for some
i
. In particular, any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
T
terminates in at most
m
steps.

This essentially follows from Theorem
5.1
and Kőnig’s lemma: there are only finitely many extremal rays we can possibly contract at each step of the MMP, so if the MMP can have an arbitrary large number of steps, we would be able to construct an MMP that does not terminate, which contradicts Corollary
5.2
. We spell out the details in the next two lemmas.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial klt pair and
X\to T
a projective morphism such that
K_{X}+\Delta
or
\Delta
is big over
T
. Then there are only finitely many possible
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-divisorial contractions or flips over
T
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.18
we may assume that
\Delta=\Delta_{0}+A
, where
\Delta_{0}\geq 0
and
A\geq 0
is an ample
\mathbb{R}
-divisor. By the cone theorem
[
42
, Theorem 3.7(2)]
, there are only finitely many
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-negative rays over
T
. In particular, there are only finitely many possible
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-divisorial contractions or flips over
T
.
∎

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial klt pair and
X\to T
a projective morphism such that
K_{X}+\Delta
or
\Delta
is big over
T
. Assume that any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
T
terminates. Then there are finitely many birational maps
\phi_{i}:X\dashrightarrow Y_{i}
over
T
,
1\leq i\leq m
, such that for any sequences of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP
\phi:X\dashrightarrow Y
over
T
, we have
\phi=\phi_{i}
for some
i
. In particular, any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
T
terminates in at most
m
steps.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.9
, there are only finitely many possible
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-divisorial contractions or flips
\phi_{i}:(X_{0},\Delta_{0}):=(X,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(X_{1}^{(j)},\Delta_{1}^{(j)})
,
1\leq j\leq m_{1}
, where
\Delta_{1}^{(j)}
is the strict transform of
\Delta_{0}
on
X_{1}^{(j)}
. If the lemma does not hold for
(X,\Delta)
, then the lemma does not hold for
(X_{1}^{(j)},\Delta_{1}^{(j)})
for some
j
. Let
(X_{1},\Delta_{1}):=(X_{1}^{(j)},\Delta_{1}^{(j)})
and repeat the same argument with
(X_{1},\Delta_{1})
in place of
(X_{0},\Delta_{0})
. Inductively, we may construct
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
,
i\geq 1
, such that

(X_{0},\Delta_{0})\dashrightarrow(X_{1},\Delta_{1})\dashrightarrow\cdots\dashrightarrow(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\dashrightarrow(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})\dashrightarrow\cdots

is an infinite sequence of
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
T
, and the lemma does not hold for each
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
. This is impossible as any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP over
T
terminates.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.8
.

This follows from Corollary
5.2
and Lemma
5.10
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

This follows from Theorem
5.8
.∎

As another application of the termination result in this section, we prove the strong Sarkisov program conjecture (
[
33
]
*Conjecture 3.2) in dimension
5
:

Corollary 5.11
.

The strong Sarkisov program holds in dimension
5
.

We refer to
[
33
]
and the reference therein for more details of the strong Sarkisov program.

Proof.

It follows from Corollary
5.2
and
[
33
]
*Corollary 3.5.
∎

6.
Explicit termination

In this section, we give explicit bounds (see Corollary
6.9
, Theorems
6.14
,
6.15
, and
6.16
) on the number of steps in various types of MMP in dimension at most
4
. This improves Theorem
5.8
, which only gives the existence of an inexplicit upper bound.

6.1.
Invariants

Before we state the result, we need to introduce a few invariants that we shall use to formulate the explicit bound and recall some of their basic properties.

Definition 6.1

(Picard number)
.

Let
X
be a smooth variety. Let
j\colon X\hookrightarrow\overline{X}
be a smooth projective compactification. We set
N^{1}(X):=\mathrm{Pic}(X)/j^{*}\mathrm{Pic}^{0}(\overline{X})
and define the
Picard number
of
X
as

\rho(X):=\dim N^{1}(X)_{\mathbb{R}}.

This is well-defined since
j^{*}\mathrm{Pic}^{0}(\overline{X})
is independent of the compactification. In general, for any normal variety
X
, we define
\rho(X):=\rho(U)
where
U
is the smooth locus of
X
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\pi\colon X\rightarrow Z
be a projective birational morphism of normal varieties. Then
\rho(X)=\rho(Z)+\ell
, where
\ell
is the number of
\pi
-exceptional divisors.

Proof.

If
X
is smooth, this follows from
[
1
, Lemma 1.6(1)]
. The general case can be reduced to this by considering a common resolution of
X
and
Z
.
∎

Definition 6.3

(Algebraic Betti number)
.

Let
X
be a variety and let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. We denote by
H^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(X)
the subspace in the singular homology group

H_{2m}(X,\mathbb{R})
generated by the the classes of
m
-dimensional complete algebraic subvarieties. We denote by
h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(X)
the dimension of
H^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(X)
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\pi\colon X\rightarrow Z
be a projective birational morphism of normal varieties such that
\dim\mathrm{Ex}(\pi)\leq m
. Then

(6.1)

h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(Z)+\min\{1,\ell\}\leq h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(X)\leq h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(Z)+\ell

where
\ell
is the number of
m
-dimensional complete irreducible components of
\mathrm{Ex}(\pi)
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we abbreviate all singular homology groups
H_{*}(-,\mathbb{R})
as
H_{*}(-)
in this proof.

As
\pi
is projective, every complete subvariety of
Z
is the image of some complete subvariety of
X
of the same dimension. Thus the natural homomorphism
\pi_{*}\colon H_{2m}(X)\to H_{2m}(Z)
induces a surjection
H^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(X)\twoheadrightarrow H^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(Z)
. In particular,
h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(Z)\leq h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(X)
. For any complete subvariety
V\subseteq X
of dimension
m
and any projective compactification
\overline{X}
of
X
, we have
[V]\neq 0\in H^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(\overline{X})
, hence
[V]\neq 0\in H^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(X)
as well. If
\pi
contracts at least one complete subvariety of dimension
m
(i.e.
\ell\geq 1
), then it follows that
\ker(\pi_{*})\neq 0
and we get
h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(Z)\leq h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(X)-1
. This proves the first inequality in (
6.1
).

Let
W=\mathrm{Ex}(\pi)
. By assumption,
\dim\pi(W)\leq m-1
, thus
H_{i}(\pi(W))=0
for any
i>2m-2
. By the long exact sequence of homology we deduce that
H_{2m}(Z)\cong H_{2m}(Z,\pi(W))
where
H_{2m}(Z,\pi(W))
is the relative homology. Since
X\setminus W\cong Z\setminus\pi(W)
, by excision we also have
H_{2m}(Z,\pi(W))\cong H_{2m}(X,W)
. Combining with the long exact sequence of homology we see that

H_{2m}(W)\to H_{2m}(X)\to H_{2m}(X,W)\cong H_{2m}(Z)

is exact. Note that
\dim H_{2m}(W)=\ell
by the Mayer–Vietoris sequence and the fact that for any variety
V
of dimension at most
m
, we have

\dim H_{2m}(V)=\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{if $V$ is complete and }\dim V=m,\\
0,&\mbox{otherwise}.\end{cases}

It follows that
\ker(\pi_{*})\leq\ell

and hence
h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(X)\leq h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2m}(Z)+\ell
. This proves the other inequality in (
6.1
).
∎

Definition 6.5
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair of dimension
n
and let
\widetilde{B}
be the normalization of
B:=\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
. We define

(6.2)

\rho(X,\Delta):=\rho(X)+\rho(\widetilde{B})+h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2n-4}(X).

Following
[
1
]
, we call an exceptional divisor
E
over
X
with
a(E,X,\Delta)<1
an
echo
of
(X,\Delta)
if its center
c_{X}(E)
is a codimension one point in the smooth locus of
B
. We also define

(6.3)

d(X,\Delta):=\#\left\{\begin{tabular}[]{c}\mbox{Exceptional prime divisor $E$ over $X$ with}\\
\mbox{$a(E,X,\Delta)<1$ that is not an echo of $(X,\Delta)$}\end{tabular}\right\}.

Note that if
(X,\Delta)
is terminal, then
d(X,\Delta)<+\infty
by
[
43
]
*Lemma (4.14.2.1) or
[
1
]
*Lemma 1.5.
Finally, we define

(6.4)

s(X,\Delta):=\min_{(Y,D)}\{\rho(Y,D)+d(Y,D)\}

where the minimum runs over all terminal pairs
(Y,D)
with a projective birational morphism
\pi\colon(Y,D)\to(X,\Delta)
such that
K_{Y}+D=\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E
for some
\pi
-exceptional
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
E\geq 0
.

Since the last invariant
s(X,\Delta)
will play a major role in controlling the number of steps in the MMP, we give an explicit upper bound of
s(X,\Delta)
at least for log smooth pairs. We also bound the invariant
e_{+}(X,\Delta)
defined after (
3.1
).

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
(X,\Delta=\sum b_{i}B_{i})
be a log smooth pair of dimension
n
. Let
N
be a positive integer. Assume that
b_{i}\leq 1-\frac{1}{N}
for all
i
,
(X,\Delta)
has at most
N
strata, each stratum has Picard number at most
N
, and
h_{2n-4}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\leq N
. Then

e_{+}(X,\Delta)<N^{n+1}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad s(X,\Delta)<3N^{2n+3}.

Proof.

The result is clear when
N=1
: in this case
\Delta=0
and
\rho(X)=h_{2n-4}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X)=1
by assumption, which gives
e_{+}(X,\Delta)=d(X,\Delta)=0
(as
(X,\Delta)
is already terminal) and
s(X,\Delta)=2
by definition. Thus we may assume that
N\geq 2
.

A
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization
(Y,D)
of
(X,\Delta)
can be constructed by repeatedly blowing up the center of some prime exceptional divisor
E
over
X
with
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq 0
until all such divisors have been extracted. Note that the center of such a divisor
E
is necessarily a stratum at each step (this follows from e.g. the calculations in
[
42
, Corollary 2.31]
). For each stratum
W\subseteq B_{J}
, every divisor
E
with center
W
and
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq 0
can also be obtained by a weighted blowup with some weights
m_{j}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
along
B_{j}
. Since

a(E,X,\Delta)=-1+\sum_{j\in J}(1-b_{j})m_{j}

for the exceptional divisor
E
of such a weighted blowup, and
b_{i}\leq 1-\frac{1}{N}
by assumption, we see that
m_{j}\leq N
and hence for each stratum
W
there are no more than
N^{n}
divisors
E
with center
W
and
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq 0
. As there at no more than
N
strata and one of them is
X
, we deduce that the
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
D
on the terminalization has less than
N^{n+1}
components, and we only need to blowup less than
N^{n+1}
times to obtain the terminalization
Y
. In particular,
e_{+}(X,\Delta)<N^{n+1}
.

At the initial stage (i.e. on
X
), the intersection of any two strata has at most
N
connected components, and this number does not increase under the blowup process. This implies that after each stratum blowup, the Picard number of a stratum can increase at most
N
, hence after
\ell
blowups, the Picard number of any stratum is at most
(\ell+1)N
. It follows that every stratum of
(Y,D)
has Picard number at most
N^{n+2}
; in particular,
\rho(Y)\leq N^{n+2}
, and if
\widetilde{B}_{Y}
is the normalization of
B_{Y}:=\mathrm{Supp}(D)
, then

\rho(\widetilde{B}_{Y})\leq N^{n+2}(N^{n+1}-1)

as
D
has less than
N^{n+1}
components. On the other hand, by
[
62
, Theorem 7.31]
, if we blowup a stratum of Picard number
\rho
, then
h_{2n-4}^{\mathrm{alg}}
increases by at most
\rho+1
. By assumption,
h_{2n-4}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\leq N
, hence

h_{2n-4}^{\mathrm{alg}}(Y)\leq h_{2n-4}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X)+(N+1)+(2N+1)+\dots+((N^{n+1}-1)N+1)<N^{2n+3}.

Putting the above together we see that

\rho(Y,D)=\rho(Y)+\rho(\widetilde{B}_{Y})+h_{2n-4}^{\mathrm{alg}}(Y)<N^{n+2}+N^{n+2}(N^{N+1}-1)+N^{2n+3}=2N^{2n+3}.

Next observe that by
[
44
, Corollary 3.2(iii)]
, every divisor
E
over
Y
with
a(E,Y,D)=a(E,X,\Delta)<1
that is not an echo of
(Y,D)
is necessarily obtained by a weighted blowup along some stratum of
(X,\Delta)
, as the assumptions of
loc. cit.
is satisfied after blowing up some strata. For each fixed stratum
W\subseteq B_{J}
, if
m_{j}
(
j\in J
) are the weights along
B_{j}
, then

\sum_{j\in J}(1-b_{j})m_{j}=1+a(E,Y,D)<2.

As
b_{i}\leq 1-\frac{1}{N}
, we have
m_{j}<2N
, hence there are at most
(2N)^{n}
divisors
E
with
a(E,X,\Delta)<1
that are centered at any given stratum. Since there are no more than
N
strata, we see that
d(Y,D)\leq 2^{n}N^{n+1}
and (since
N\geq 2
)

s(X,\Delta)\leq\rho(Y,D)+d(Y,D)<2N^{2n+3}+2^{n}N^{n+1}<3N^{2n+3},

which concludes the proof.
∎

6.2.
Terminal case

Next we prove an explicit bound on the number of steps in a special type of MMP on terminal pairs, which includes all terminal flips in dimension
3
or
4
.

We start with some general setup. For a terminal pair
(X,\Delta)
of dimension
n
, write
\Delta=\sum_{i=1}^{k}b_{i}B_{i}
where
0<b_{k}<\dots<b_{1}<1
and the
B_{i}
’s are reduced divisors without common components, and let
\widetilde{B}_{i}
be the normalization of
B_{i}
. Set
b_{0}:=1
,
b_{k+1}:=0
, and let

(6.5)

S:=[0,1]\cap\sum_{i=0}^{k}b_{i}\mathbb{N}\subseteq\mathbb{R}.

In particular,
S=\{0,1\}
if
\Delta=0
. We define a sequence of topological invariants as follows.

(6.6)

\rho_{i}:=\rho_{i}(X,\Delta):=\begin{cases}\rho(X),&\mbox{if }i=0,\\
\rho(\widetilde{B}_{i}),&\mbox{if }1\leq i\leq k,\mbox{ and}\\
h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2n-4}(X),&\mbox{if }i=k+1.\end{cases}

By definition,
\rho(X,\Delta)=\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}\rho_{i}(X,\Delta)
(
cf.
(
6.2
)).
For each
i=1,\dots,k+1
, we also define the
i
-th (weighted) difficulty invariant by

(6.7)

d_{i}:=d_{i}(X,\Delta):=\sum_{\eta\in S,\,\eta\geq b_{i}}\#\left\{\begin{tabular}[]{c}\mbox{Exceptional divisors $E$ over $X$ with}\\
\mbox{$a(E,X,\Delta)<1-\eta$ whose center $c_{X}(E)$ is not a}\\
\mbox{codimension one point in the smooth locus of $\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)$}\end{tabular}\right\}.

Clearly
d_{1}\leq d_{2}\leq\cdots\leq d_{k+1}\leq|S|\cdot d(X,\Delta)
, where
d(X,\Delta)
is defined in (
6.3
). The importance of these invariants comes from the following result.

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminal pair of dimension
n\in\{3,4\}
. Then in an
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP that does not contract any component of
\Delta
, the sequence
(\rho_{0},d_{1},\rho_{1},d_{2},\dots,d_{k+1},\rho_{k+1})
is strictly decreasing in lexicographic order.

Proof.

This follows from the proof
[
21
,
22
]
that such MMP terminates.
∎

This suggests that if we define an “
M
-adic difficulty” by

(6.8)

\displaystyle\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=0}^{k+1}M^{2k+2-2i}\rho_{i}(X,\Delta)+\sum_{i=1}^{k+1}M^{2k+3-2i}d_{i}(X,\Delta)

\displaystyle=M^{2k+2}\rho_{0}+M^{2k+1}d_{1}+M^{2k}\rho_{1}+\dots+Md_{k+1}+\rho_{k+1},

where
M
is a positive integer, then it is reasonable to expect that
\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)
is also strictly decreasing in the MMP in Theorem 6.7 when
M\gg 0
. The main result of this subsection is an explicit form of such a statement.

Theorem 6.8
.

Let
(X,\Delta=\sum_{i=1}^{k}b_{i}B_{i})
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminal pair of dimension
n
with
b_{k}<\dots<b_{1}
. Set
b_{0}=1
,
b_{k+1}=0
and
S=[0,1]\cap\sum_{i=0}^{k}b_{i}\mathbb{N}
as above. Then for any

M\geq\left(2+\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil\cdot\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\right)\cdot|S|,

the invariant
\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)
is non-increasing in any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP that does not contract any component of
\Delta
. Moreover, it strictly decreases in the MMP if
n\in\{3,4\}
.

Corollary 6.9
.

Let
(X,\Delta=\sum_{i=1}^{k}b_{i}B_{i})
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminal pair of dimension
n\in\{3,4\}
with
b_{k}<\dots<b_{1}
. Let
b_{0}=1
,
b_{k+1}=0
and
S=[0,1]\cap\sum_{i=0}^{k}b_{i}\mathbb{N}
. Then every
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP that does not contract any component of
\Delta
terminates after at most

\left(2+\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil\cdot\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\right)^{2k+2}|S|^{2k+2}(\rho(X,\Delta)+d(X,\Delta))

steps, where
\rho(X,\Delta)
and
d(X,\Delta)
are defined in Definition
6.5
.

The remaining part of this subsection is devoted to proving the two results above.

Proof of Theorem
6.8
.

By induction, it suffices to prove that
\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)\geq\delta_{M}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
for any step
\varphi\colon(X,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
of the
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP which does not contract any component of
\Delta
, and that the inequality is strict if
n\in\{3,4\}
. Let
\rho^{\prime}_{i}:=\rho_{i}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
and
d^{\prime}_{i}:=d_{i}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
.

First assume that
\varphi
is a flip. Let
f\colon X\to Z
be the flipping contraction and
f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to Z
the flip. Let
B^{\prime}_{i}
be the strict transform of
B_{i}
on
X^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{i}
its normalization, and let
\bar{B}_{i}
be the normalization of
f(B_{i})=f^{\prime}(B^{\prime}_{i})\subseteq Z
. For ease of notation we also denote the induced morphism
\widetilde{B}_{i}\to\bar{B}_{i}
and
\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{i}\to\bar{B}_{i}
by
f
and
f^{\prime}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{B}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{B}_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f(B_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\supseteq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\subseteq}

We have
\rho_{0}=\rho^{\prime}_{0}
since
\varphi
is small. To compare the other invariants
\rho^{\prime}_{i},d^{\prime}_{i}
with
\rho_{i},d_{i}
, we introduce two intermediate sequences as follows.

(6.9)

\bar{\rho}_{i}:=\begin{cases}\rho(\bar{B}_{i}),&\mbox{if }1\leq i\leq k,\\
h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2n-4}(Z),&\mbox{if }i=k+1.\end{cases}

(6.10)

\bar{d}_{i}:=\sum_{\eta\in S,\eta\geq b_{i}}\#\left\{\begin{tabular}[]{c}\mbox{Exceptional divisors $E$ over $X^{\prime}$ with}\\
\mbox{$a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})<1-\eta$ whose center $c_{X}(E)$ on $X$ is not a}\\
\mbox{codimension one point in the smooth locus of $\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)$}\end{tabular}\right\}.

Note that in the definition of
\bar{d}_{i}
, the discrepancy is computed with respect to
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
while the center is taken on
X
. It is clear that
\rho_{i}\geq\bar{\rho}_{i}
for all
i=1,\dots,k+1
. Since
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
for all prime divisors
E
over
X
by the negativity lemma, we also have
d_{i}\geq\bar{d}_{i}
for all
i=1,\dots,k+1
. We claim that

(6.11)

d^{\prime}_{i}-\bar{d}_{i}\leq(M-1)\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j}),\,\mbox{ and }

(6.12)

\rho^{\prime}_{i}-\bar{\rho}_{i}\leq(M-1)\left(d_{i}-\bar{d}_{i}+\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})\right).

Roughly speaking, the claim says that the sequence
(\rho_{0},d_{1},\rho_{1},d_{2},\dots,d_{k+1},\rho_{k+1})
behaves like the digits of
M
-adic numbers: each time a higher digit drops by
1
, the lower digits increase by at most
M-1
. Taking (
6.11
) and (
6.12
) for granted, Lemma
6.10
then implies that
\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)\geq\delta_{M}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
. Moreover, equality holds if and only if
d^{\prime}_{i}=d_{i}
for all
i=1,\dots,k+1
and
\rho^{\prime}_{j}=\rho_{j}
for all
j=0,1,\dots,k+1
. This is not possible in dimension
3
and
4
by Theorem
6.7
, thus
\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)>\delta_{M}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
when
n\in\{3,4\}
.

We now focus on the proof of (
6.11
) and (
6.12
).

Proof of (
6.11
).

Let
E
be an exceptional divisor that has more contributions to
d^{\prime}_{i}
than to
\bar{d}_{i}
. Let
\xi=c_{X}(E)
(resp.
\xi^{\prime}=c_{X^{\prime}}(E)
) be its center on
X
(resp.
X^{\prime}
). Then
\xi^{\prime}
is not a codimension one point in the smooth locus of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})
and
a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})<1-b_{i}
, otherwise
E
has no contribution to
d^{\prime}_{i}
. On the other hand, since
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
, we see that
\xi
must be a codimension one point in the smooth locus of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
, otherwise it cannot have more contributions to
d^{\prime}_{i}
. Thus
\xi
is contained in the exceptional locus of
f
. We also have
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})<1-b_{i}
, hence
\xi\in B_{j}
for some
j<i
by e.g.
[
1
]
*Lemma 1.5. By Lemma
6.2
, there are at most
\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j}
components of dimension
n-2
in the exceptional locus of
f|_{B_{j}}
, and by
[
1
]
*Lemma 1.5, over the generic point of every such component that is contained in the smooth locus of
B_{j}
there are at most
\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{j}}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil
divisors
E
with
a(E,X,\Delta)<1
. It follows that there are at most

\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\cdot\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})

exceptional divisors
E
that contribute positively to
d^{\prime}_{i}-\bar{d}_{i}
. Note that every such divisor
E
is counted at most
|S|
times in
d^{\prime}_{i}
. Thus

d^{\prime}_{i}-\bar{d}_{i}\leq|S|\cdot\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\cdot\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})\leq(M-1)\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j}),

which gives (
6.11
).
∎

Proof of (
6.12
).

For notational convenience, set
\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{k+1}=B^{\prime}_{k+1}=X^{\prime}
. By Lemmas
6.2
and
6.4
, we know that
\rho^{\prime}_{i}-\bar{\rho}_{i}
is at most the number of irreducible components of dimension
n-2
in the exceptional locus of
f^{\prime}|_{\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{i}}
. We first count the number of irreducible components of dimension
n-2
in the exceptional locus of
f^{\prime}|_{B^{\prime}_{i}}
(that is, before passing to the normalization). Let
W
be one such component. Note that
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is terminal, hence
X^{\prime}
is smooth at the generic point of
W
. Let
E
be the exceptional divisor obtained by blowing up
W
near the generic point of
W
on
X^{\prime}
. Then by
[
21
]
*Lemma 2.2, we have

(6.13)

a(E,X,\Delta)<a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})=1-\eta

for some
b_{i}\leq\eta\in S
.

Let
\xi=c_{X}(E)
be the center of
E
on
X
. Then
\xi\in\mathrm{Ex}(f)
since
c_{X^{\prime}}(E)\in W\subseteq\mathrm{Ex}(f^{\prime})
. If
\xi
is not a codimension one point in the smooth locus of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
, then (
6.13
) implies that
E
has more contributions to
d_{i}
than to
\bar{d}_{i}
, and there are at most
d_{i}-\bar{d}_{i}
such divisors. If
\xi
is a codimension one point in the smooth locus of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
, then by (
6.13
) and
[
1
]
*Lemma 1.5 we see that
\xi\in B_{j}
for some
1\leq j<i
. Since
\xi\in\mathrm{Ex}(f)
, it is also the generic point of a component
V
of dimension
n-2
in the exceptional locus of
f
. Combined with Lemma
6.2
, we deduce that there are at most
\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})
such components
V
. Moreover, once
V
is fixed, there are at most
\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{j}}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil
divisors
E
centered at the generic point of
V
such that
a(E,X,\Delta)<1
. Putting these together, and since
W
is uniquely determined by
E
as its center, we see that there are at most

d_{i}-\bar{d}_{i}+\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})

components
W
of dimension
n-2
in the exceptional locus of
f^{\prime}|_{B^{\prime}_{i}}
. When
i=k+1
this already gives an upper bound of
\rho^{\prime}_{i}-\bar{\rho}_{i}
by Lemma
6.4
. If
1\leq i\leq k
, then since
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is terminal, we have
\mathrm{mult}_{W}\Delta^{\prime}<1
and hence
\mathrm{mult}_{W}B^{\prime}_{i}<\frac{1}{b_{i}}
. It follows that the preimage of
W
in the normalization
\widetilde{B}^{\prime}_{i}
has at most
\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{i}}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil
components. In other words, the contribution to
\rho^{\prime}_{i}-\bar{\rho}_{i}
by each component
W
is at most
\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{i}-\bar{\rho}_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil\left(d_{i}-\bar{d}_{i}+\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})\right)

\displaystyle\leq(M-1)\left(d_{i}-\bar{d}_{i}+\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})\right)

as desired.
∎

As discussed before, this concludes the proof of the theorem when
\varphi
is a flip.

Next assume that
\varphi\colon(X,\Delta)\to(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is a divisorial contraction. Then clearly
\rho^{\prime}_{0}=\rho_{0}-1
and
\rho^{\prime}_{i}\leq\rho_{i}
for all
i\geq 1
. Since none of the components of
\Delta
is contracted, the pair
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
remains terminal. Set
\bar{\rho}_{i}:=\rho^{\prime}_{i}
and
\bar{d}_{i}:=d_{i}
. Similar to the previous case, we claim that

(6.14)

d^{\prime}_{i}-\bar{d}_{i}\leq(M-1)\sum_{0\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j}),

which implies, again by Theorem
6.7
and Lemma
6.10
, that
\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)\geq\delta_{M}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
, with strict inequality when
n\in\{3,4\}
.

To see this claim, let
F
be the (unique) exceptional divisor of
\varphi
and let
E
be an exceptional divisor over
X^{\prime}
that contributes more to
d^{\prime}_{i}
than to
\bar{d}_{i}=d_{i}
. Let
\xi=c_{X}(E)
(resp.
\xi^{\prime}=c_{X^{\prime}}(E)
) be the center of
E
on
X
(resp.
X^{\prime}
). Then
\xi^{\prime}
is not a codimension one point of the smooth locus of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta^{\prime})
, and
a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})<1-b_{i}
. Since
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
, in order for the divisor
E
to contribute less to
d_{i}
, there are only two possibilities: either
E=F
(so it is not exceptional over
X
), or
E
is exceptional over
X
but its center
\xi
is a codimension one point of the smooth locus of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
. In both cases we necessarily have
\xi\in\mathrm{Ex}(\varphi)=F
. Let us analyze the latter situation more carefully.

Since
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq a(E,X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})<1-b_{i}
, by
[
1
]
*Lemma 1.5 we know that
\xi\in B_{j}
for some
1\leq j<i
. Note that
\xi^{\prime}=\varphi(\xi)
. If
\xi^{\prime}
is a point of codimension at least
3
in
X^{\prime}
, then
W=\overline{\{\xi\}}
is a component of dimension
n-2
in the exceptional locus of
\varphi|_{B_{j}}
. There are at most
\rho_{j}-\rho^{\prime}_{j}=\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j}
such components by Lemma
6.2
. Once
W
is fixed, by
[
1
]
*Lemma 1.5, there are at most
\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{j}}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil
exceptional divisors
E
with center
W
and
a(E,X,\Delta)<1
. As
j
varies these give at most

\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})

candidates of the divisor
E
in total. If
\xi^{\prime}
has codimension
2
in
X^{\prime}
, then
\xi^{\prime}
is the generic point of
\varphi(F)
(because
\xi\in F
and
\varphi(F)
has codimension at least
2
in
X
) and
\varphi|_{B_{j}}
is finite around
\xi
. As
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is terminal, we get
\mathrm{mult}_{\xi^{\prime}}\Delta^{\prime}<1
and thus
\mathrm{mult}_{\xi^{\prime}}(B^{\prime}_{1}+\dots+B^{\prime}_{j})<\frac{1}{b_{j}}\leq\frac{1}{b_{k}}
. It follows that the preimage of
\xi^{\prime}
in the normalization
\widetilde{B}_{1}\cup\dots\cup\widetilde{B}_{j}
has at most
\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil
components, giving rise to at most
\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil
possibilities of
\xi
. For each such choice of
\xi
, there are at most
\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{j}}\right\rceil\leq\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil
divisors
E
with center
\xi
such that
a(E,X,\Delta)<1
(by
[
1
]
*Lemma 1.5 as before). Putting these together, we conclude that there are at most

\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\cdot\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil+\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})=\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\cdot\left(\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil+\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})\right)

exceptional divisors over
X
that contribute more to
d^{\prime}_{i}
than to
d_{i}
. Recall that
\rho_{0}-\rho^{\prime}_{0}=1
and there is also the option that
E=F
. As every divisor
E
is counted at most
|S|
times in
d^{\prime}_{i}
, we finally deduce that

d^{\prime}_{i}-\bar{d}_{i}\leq|S|\cdot\left(1+\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\cdot\left(\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil+\sum_{1\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j})\right)\right)\leq(M-1)\sum_{0\leq j<i}(\rho_{j}-\bar{\rho}_{j}).

This proves the claim and hence of the theorem as well.
∎

The following elementary lemma has been used in the proof above.

Lemma 6.10
.

Let
a_{i},b_{i},c_{i}\in\mathbb{R}\,\,(i=0,\dots,m)
and
M>1
be such that
a_{i}\geq b_{i}
and
c_{i}-b_{i}\leq(M-1)\sum_{0\leq j<i}(a_{j}-b_{j})
for all
i
. Then

\sum_{i=0}^{m}M^{-i}c_{i}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{m}M^{-i}a_{i}

with equality if and only if
a_{i}=b_{i}=c_{i}
for all
i
.

In the applications above, the sequence
a_{i}
(resp.
b_{i}
, resp.
c_{i}
) represents the sequence
(\rho_{0},d_{1},\dots,d_{k+1},\rho_{k+1})
(resp.
(\bar{\rho}_{0},\bar{d}_{1},\dots,\bar{d}_{k+1},\bar{\rho}_{k+1})
, resp.
(\rho^{\prime}_{0},d^{\prime}_{1},\dots,d^{\prime}_{k+1},\rho^{\prime}_{k+1})
).

Proof.

We may assume that
b_{i}=0
by replacing
a_{i}
(resp.
c_{i}
) with
a_{i}-b_{i}
(resp.
c_{i}-b_{i}
). The assumption then becomes
a_{i}\geq 0
and
c_{i}\leq(M-1)\sum_{0\leq j<i}a_{j}
. It follows that
M^{-i}c_{i}\leq(1-\frac{1}{M})\sum_{0\leq j<i}M^{1-i}a_{j}
, hence

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{m}M^{-i}c_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{M}\right)\sum_{i=0}^{m}\sum_{0\leq j<i}M^{1-i}a_{j}\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{M}\right)\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\sum_{i=j}^{+\infty}M^{-i}a_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}M^{-j}a_{j}\leq\sum_{j=0}^{m}M^{-j}a_{j}.

The second and the last equality hold if and only if
a_{i}=0
for all
i
. If this is the case, then the first equality holds if and only if
c_{i}=(M-1)\sum_{0\leq j<i}a_{j}=0
for all
i
.
∎

To prove Corollary
6.9
, we need another elementary statement.

Lemma 6.11
.

We have
\rho(X,\Delta)+d(X,\Delta)\leq\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)
. Assume that
M\geq|S|+1
. Then we also have

(6.15)

\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)\leq M^{2k+2}(\rho(X,\Delta)+d(X,\Delta)).

Proof.

The first inequality is clear from the definition (
6.8
) of
\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)
. If
M\geq|S|+1
, then as
d_{i}\leq|S|\cdot d(X.\Delta)
for all
i
, from (
6.8
) we also have

\displaystyle\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)

\displaystyle\leq M^{2k+2}\rho(X,\Delta)+(M^{2k+1}+M^{2k-1}+\dots+M)\cdot|S|\cdot d(X,\Delta)

\displaystyle\leq M^{2k+2}\rho(X,\Delta)+(M^{2k+1}+M^{2k-1}+\dots+M)\cdot(M-1)\cdot d(X,\Delta)

\displaystyle\leq M^{2k+2}(\rho(X,\Delta)+d(X,\Delta)),

which gives the second inequality.
∎

Proof of Corollary
6.9
.

Since
\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)\in\mathbb{N}
by definition, we deduce from Theorem
6.8
that the MMP terminates in at most
\delta_{M}(X,\Delta)
steps, where

M:=\left(2+\left\lceil\frac{1}{b_{k}}\right\rceil\cdot\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-b_{1}}\right\rceil\right)\cdot|S|.

The result then follows from Lemma
6.11
.
∎

6.3.
MMP with discrete discrepancies

Our next goal is to give explicit bounds for klt MMP in dimension
3
, or in dimension
4
when one of
K_{X}+\Delta
or
\Delta
is big. A common feature in these cases is that the log discrepancies belong to some fixed finite set during the MMP, see Section
3
. To give a more uniform treatment, we first prove an explicit termination statement by fixing this set. See Definition
6.5
for the definition of
s(X,\Delta)
and
e(X,\Delta)
that appear in the statement below.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
S\subseteq[0,1]
be a finite set such that
0,1\in S
and for any
a,b\in S
with
a+b\leq 1
, we have
a+b\in S
.
Let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt pair of dimension
n\in\{3,4\}
. Let

(6.16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\Delta)=:(X_{0},\Delta_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{1},\Delta_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{i},\Delta_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}

be an
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP such that for any divisor
E
over
X
and any
i
with
a(E,X_{i},\Delta_{i})<0
, we have
-a(E,X_{i},\Delta_{i})\in S
.
Then this MMP terminates after at most

M^{2(k+1)^{2}|S|}s(X,\Delta)

steps, where
k=e_{+}(X,\Delta)
and
M=\left(2+\left\lceil\frac{1}{\min S\setminus\{0\}}\right\rceil\cdot\left\lceil\frac{1}{1-\max S\setminus\{1\}}\right\rceil\right)\cdot|S|
.

Before proving this we make an elementary observation.

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a terminal pair and let
0\leq D\leq\Delta
be an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor such that
K_{X}+D
is
\mathbb{R}
-Cartier. Then
\rho(X,D)\leq\rho(X,\Delta)
and
d(X,D)\leq d(X,\Delta)
.

Proof.

As
0\leq D\leq\Delta
, every component of
D
is also a component of
\Delta
, hence the first inequality is clear from the definition. Since
a(E,X,\Delta)\leq a(E,X,D)
holds for any prime divisor
E
over
X
, to prove the second inequality it suffices to show that if
E
is an exceptional divisor with
a(E,X,D)<1
and it is not an echo of
(X,D)
, then it is also not an echo of
(X,\Delta)
. Suppose that
E
is an echo for
(X,\Delta)
, then its center
\xi
is a codimension one smooth point of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
. Note that in this case
\xi\in\mathrm{Supp}(D)
, otherwise
a(E,X,D)=a(E,X)\geq 1
since
X
is terminal and hence smooth at
\xi
. It follows that
\xi
is also a codimension one smooth point of
\mathrm{Supp}(D)
and hence an echo for
(X,D)
, a contradiction. This gives the second inequality.
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.12
.

Let
\pi\colon(Y,D)\to(X,\Delta)
be a projective birational morphism from a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminal pair such that
K_{Y}+D=\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E
for some
\pi
-exceptional
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
E\geq 0
. In other words,
\mathrm{mult}_{F}(D)\geq\min\{0,-a(F,X,\Delta)\}
for all prime divisors
F
on
Y
. We need to prove that the MMP (
6.16
) terminates after at most
M^{2(k+1)^{2}|S|}(\rho(Y,D)+d(Y,D))
steps. By Lemma
6.13
, we may decrease the coefficients of
D
and assume that

\mathrm{mult}_{F}(D)=\min\{0,-a(F,X,\Delta)\}

for all prime divisors
F
on
Y
. In particular, we may assume that
\mathrm{Coeff}(D)\subseteq S
.

By Lemma
2.17
, we can lift the given
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP to a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y,D)=:(Y_{0},D_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y_{1},D_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Y_{i},D_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\Delta)=:(X_{0},\Delta_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{1},\Delta_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{i},\Delta_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{0}=\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{i}}

where each
\pi_{i}
(
i\geq 1
) is a
\mathbb{Q}
-factorial terminalization of
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
and each
\psi_{i}
(
i\geq 0
) is composed of a sequence of
(K_{Y_{i}}+B_{i})
-MMP where
B_{i}=(\psi_{i}^{-1})_{*}D_{i+1}\leq D_{i}
. In particular,
\psi_{i}
does not contract any component of
B_{i}
. Let
\delta_{i}:=\delta_{M}(Y_{i},D_{i})
which is defined by (
6.8
). At each step we have two possibilities:

(1)

B_{i}=D_{i}
. In this case
\delta_{i+1}\leq\delta_{i}-1
by Theorem
6.8
.

(2)

B_{i}\neq D_{i}
. By Lemma
6.13
and Lemma
6.11
, we have
\delta_{M}(Y_{i},B_{i})\leq M^{2k+2}\delta_{M}(Y_{i},D_{i})
, hence Theorem
6.8
gives
\delta_{i+1}\leq\delta_{M}(Y_{i},B_{i})\leq M^{2k+2}\delta_{i}
(note that in this case it might be possible that
Y_{i+1}=Y_{i}
).

By construction
\mathrm{Supp}(D_{i})
has only
k
components, and
\mathrm{Coeff}(B_{i})\cup\mathrm{Coeff}(D_{i})\subseteq S
for all
i
, hence the second case can only happen at most
k\cdot|S|
times since we always have
0\leq B_{i}\leq D_{i}
. If we rescale the sequence
\delta_{0},\delta_{1},\dots
by setting
\tilde{\delta}_{i}:=M^{(2k+2)\ell_{i}}\delta_{i}
where
\ell_{i}=\#\{j\geq i\,|\,B_{j}\neq D_{j}\}
, then the above analysis gives
\tilde{\delta}_{i+1}\leq\tilde{\delta}_{i}
for all
i
and the equality holds at most
k\cdot|S|
times. From here we see that the MMP (
6.16
) must terminate after at most

\tilde{\delta}_{0}\leq M^{(2k+2)k\cdot|S|}\delta_{0}+k\cdot|S|\leq M^{2(k+1)^{2}|S|}(\rho(Y,D)+d(Y,D))

steps, where the last inequality is by Lemma
6.11
. This proves the desired result.
∎

We now put together the results obtained in this section and give some concrete applications. We shall only consider MMP that starts with a log smooth pair, since for any klt pair
(X,\Delta)
, we can always choose a log resolution
\pi\colon Y\to X
and a log smooth klt pair
(Y,D)
such that
K_{Y}+D\geq\pi^{*}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E
for some
\pi
-exceptional
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
E\geq 0
, and any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP can be lifted to a
(K_{Y}+D)
-MMP by Lemma
2.15
. Moreover, if
K_{X}+\Delta
(resp.
\Delta
) is big, then we can choose the log smooth model
(Y,D)
so that
K_{Y}+D
(resp.
D
) is big.

The first application is about fourfold general type MMPs.

Theorem 6.14
.

Let
N
be a positive integer and let
(X,\Delta)
be a projective log smooth klt pair of dimension
4
such that
N\Delta
has integer coefficients. Assume that

(1)

(X,\Delta)
has at most
N
strata, each stratum has Picard number at most
N
, and
h_{4}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\leq N
,

(2)

K_{X}+\Delta
is big,
\mathrm{vol}(K_{X}+\Delta)\geq\frac{1}{N}
, and
((K_{X}+\Delta)\cdot H^{3})\leq N
for some very ample divisor
H
on
X
.

Then any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP terminates after at most
M^{M}
steps, where
M:=(2N)^{9}!
.

Proof.

By Corollary
3.20
, we know that in any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP, the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor is at most
(2^{8}N^{9})!
. Since
N\Delta
has integer coefficients, we see that the assumptions of Lemma
6.12
are satisfied if we set

S:=[0,1]\cap\frac{1}{N\cdot(2^{8}N^{9})!}\mathbb{R}.

Let
N_{1}:=N\cdot(2^{8}N^{9})!
. Then
|S|=N_{1}+1
and
\min(S\setminus\{0\})=1-\max(S\setminus\{1\})=\frac{1}{N_{1}}
. By Lemma
6.6
, we also have
e(X,\Delta)\leq N^{5}-1
and
s(X,\Delta)\leq 3N^{11}
. Thus by Lemma
6.12
we see that the MMP terminates after at most

(2N_{1}+N_{1}^{3})^{2N^{10}(N_{1}+1)}\cdot 3N^{11}\leq M^{M}

steps.
∎

The same argument also works when the boundary
\Delta
is big.

Theorem 6.15
.

Let
N
be a positive integer and let
(X,\Delta)
be a projective log smooth klt pair of dimension
4
such that
N\Delta
has integer coefficients. Assume that

(1)

(X,\Delta)
has at most
N
strata, each stratum has Picard number at most
N
, and
h_{4}^{\mathrm{alg}}(X)\leq N
,

(2)

\Delta
is big,
\mathrm{vol}(\Delta)\geq\frac{1}{N}
, and
(\Delta\cdot H^{3})\leq N
for some very ample divisor
H
on
X
.

Then any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP terminates after at most
M^{M}
steps, where
M:=(2N)^{9}!
.

Proof.

This follows from the same proof of Theorem
6.14
, replacing Corollary
3.20
by Corollary
3.18
.
∎

Finally, we give an explicit bound on the length of threefold MMPs. Is is not clear if such an explicit termination should be expected in dimension
\geq 4
, see
[
57
, Example 12]
for some discussion.

Theorem 6.16
.

Let
N
be a positive integer and let
(X,\Delta)
be a projective log smooth klt pair of dimension
3
such that
N\Delta
has integer coefficients,
(X,\Delta)
has at most
N
strata, and each stratum has Picard number at most
N
. Then any
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP terminates after at most

3(N\cdot M!)^{8N^{8}(M!+1)}

steps, where
M:=((N^{5}+2N)!)^{2^{N^{4}}}
.

Proof.

Since
N\Delta
has integer coefficients, any positive discrepancy of
(X,\Delta)
is at least
\frac{1}{N}
. For smooth threefolds we also have
h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2}(X)=\rho(X)
(indeed, the invariant
h^{\mathrm{alg}}_{2}(X)
does not play any role in proving the termination of
3
-fold MMP, but we don’t need this fact). By Lemma
6.6
, we see that
e(X,\Delta)\leq N^{4}-1
and
s(X,\Delta)\leq 3N^{9}
. Thus by Corollary
3.22
, the Cartier index of any
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier Weil divisor is at most
M
. Then the assumptions of Lemma
6.12
are satisfied by
S:=[0,1]\cap\frac{1}{N\cdot M!}\mathbb{R}
, and it follows that the MMP terminates after at most

((2+(N\cdot M!)^{2})\cdot N\cdot M!)^{2N^{8}(M!+1)}\cdot 3N^{9}\leq 3(N\cdot M!)^{8N^{8}(M!+1)}

steps.
∎

Appendix A
Boundedness of models implies termination

In this appendix, we prove that if the pairs that appear in a log canonical MMP belong to a bounded family, then the MMP must terminate after finitely many steps. As a corollary, we show that in any sequence of a log canonical MMP the pairs are mutually non-isomorphic, answering
[
46
]
*Problem 82.

Theorem A.1
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a log canonical pair and let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X,\Delta)=:(X_{0},\Delta_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{1},\Delta_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X_{i},\Delta_{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots,}

be a sequence of steps of a
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP. Assume that the set of pairs
\{(X_{i},\Delta_{i})\,|\,i\in\mathbb{N}\}
is bounded. Then this MMP terminates after finitely many steps.

Here the boundedness assumption means that there exists an
\mathbb{R}
-Gorenstein family of pairs (see Definition
2.22
)
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{B}
that contains every
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
as a fiber.

Corollary A.2
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a log canonical pair and let
(X,\Delta)\dashrightarrow(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
be a sequence of steps of some
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP. Then
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is not isomorphic to
(X,\Delta)
.

Before we give the proof, let us recall Batyrev’s stringy
E
-function (see e.g.
[
7
]
*Definition 3.7 or
[
61
]
*Definition 7.7). First let
(X,\Delta)
be a klt log smooth sub-pair. Let
D=\cup_{i\in I}D_{i}
be a simple normal crossing divisor on
X
that contains every irreducible component of
\mathrm{Supp}(\Delta)
, and write
\Delta=\sum_{i\in I}a_{i}D_{i}
. Note that
a_{i}>-1
for all
i
by the klt assumption. Then we define the stringy
E
-function of the sub-pair
(X,\Delta)
as

E_{\mathrm{st}}(X,\Delta;u,v):=\sum_{J\subseteq I}H(D_{J}^{\circ};u,v)\prod_{j\in J}\frac{uv-1}{(uv)^{1+a_{j}}-1},

where
D_{J}^{\circ}:=\cap_{j\in J}D_{j}\setminus(\cup_{j\not\in J}D_{j})
(with the understanding that
D_{\emptyset}^{\circ}=X\setminus\mathrm{Supp}(D)
and
\prod_{j\in J}=1
if
J=\emptyset
), and
H(D_{J}^{\circ};u,v)
is the Hodge-Deligne polynomial of
D_{J}^{\circ}
, which is defined by

H(U;u,v):=\sum_{i,p,q}(-1)^{i}h^{p,q}(H^{i}_{c}(U,\mathbb{C}))u^{p}v^{q}

for any algebraic variety
U
. It is not hard to see that the definition of the stringy
E
-function is independent of the choice of
D
.

In general, let
(X,\Delta)
be a log sub-pair, let
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}
be an ideal sheaf, and let
c\in\mathbb{R}
be such that
(X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c})
is klt, in the sense that

A_{X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c}}(F):=A_{X,\Delta}(F)-c\cdot\mathrm{ord}_{E}(\mathfrak{a})>0

for all prime divisor
F
over
X
. Note that we allow
c
and the coefficient of
\Delta
to be negative. Let
\pi\colon(Y,D)\to(X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a})
be a crepant log resolution. In other words,

\mathrm{Ex}(\pi)+\mathrm{Supp}(\pi^{-1}_{*}\Delta)+\pi^{-1}\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{O}_{X}/\mathfrak{a})

is a simple normal crossing divisor on
Y
, and

-\mathrm{mult}_{F}D=a(F,X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c}):=a(F,X,\Delta)-c\cdot\mathrm{ord}_{F}(\mathfrak{a})

for every prime divisor
F
on
Y
. Then we define the stringy
E
-function of the klt sub-pair
(X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c})
as

E_{\mathrm{st}}(X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c};u,v):=E_{\mathrm{st}}(Y,D;u,v).

By
[
7
]
*Theorems 3.6 and 3.8, the definition is independent of the crepant log resolution
(Y,D)
.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
(X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c})
and
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\mathfrak{b}^{d})
be klt sub-pairs and let
\varphi\colon X\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}
be a birational map. Assume that:

(1)

There exist proper birational morphisms
f\colon Y\to X
and
f^{\prime}\colon Y\to X^{\prime}
such that
f^{\prime}=\varphi\circ f
, and

(2)

A_{X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c}}(F)\leq A_{X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\mathfrak{b}^{d}}(F)
for all divisors
F
over
X
and the inequality is strict for some
F
.

Then for
u,v\gg 0
we have

E_{\mathrm{st}}(X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c};u,v)>E_{\mathrm{st}}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\mathfrak{b}^{d};u,v).

Proof.

Let
Y
be a common log resolution of
(X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c})
and
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\mathfrak{b}^{d})
and let
D=\cup_{i\in I}D_{i}
be an SNC divisor on
Y
that contains the strict transforms of
\Delta
and
\Delta^{\prime}
, the preimage of
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{O}_{X}/\mathfrak{a})
and
\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{b})
, and all the exceptional divisors over
X
or
X^{\prime}
. Let
a_{i}=A_{X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c}}(D_{i})
and
a^{\prime}_{i}=A_{X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\mathfrak{b}^{d}}(D_{i})
.
By assumption we have
a^{\prime}_{i}\geq a_{i}>0
and the inequality is strict for some
i
. Without loss of generality we may assume that
a^{\prime}_{1}>a_{1}
. Note that the Hodge-Deligne polynomials have positive leading coefficients by
[
55
, Theorem 5.39]
. Then for any
u,v\gg 0
and any
J\subseteq I
, we have

H(D_{J}^{\circ};u,v)\prod_{j\in J}\frac{uv-1}{(uv)^{a_{j}}-1}\geq H(D_{J}^{\circ};u,v)\prod_{j\in J}\frac{uv-1}{(uv)^{a^{\prime}_{j}}-1}.

Moreover, the inequality is strict when
J=\{1\}
. By the definition of the stringy
E
-function, this implies that

E_{\mathrm{st}}(X,\Delta+\mathfrak{a}^{c};u,v)>E_{\mathrm{st}}(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}+\mathfrak{b}^{d};u,v)

when
u,v\gg 0
.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{X}}\to\mathcal{B}
be a projective morphism. Let
\overline{\mathcal{D}}
be an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor on
\overline{\mathcal{X}}
such that
(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\overline{\mathcal{D}})
is log smooth and klt, and every stratum of
(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\overline{\mathcal{D}})
is smooth over
\mathcal{B}
. Let
\mathcal{X}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{X}}
be an open subset that is a union of some open strata of
(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\overline{\mathcal{D}})
, and let
\mathcal{D}=\overline{\mathcal{D}}|_{\mathcal{X}}
. Then the stringy
E
-function
E_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathcal{D}_{b};u,v)
is constant in
b\in\mathcal{B}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that for any open stratum
\mathcal{W}^{\circ}
of
(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\overline{\mathcal{D}})
, the Hodge-Deligne polynomial
H(\mathcal{W}^{\circ}_{b};u,v)
of
