\mathcal{W}^{\circ}_{b}:=\mathcal{W}^{\circ}\cap\overline{\mathcal{X}}_{b}
is independent of
b\in\mathcal{B}
. We prove this by Noetherian induction. To this end, let
\mathcal{W}:=\overline{\mathcal{W}^{\circ}}
which is a stratum of
(\overline{\mathcal{X}},\overline{\mathcal{D}})
. There is nothing to prove when
\mathcal{W}=\emptyset
. For the inductive step, note that by Deligne’s mixed Hodge theory, we have

(A.1)

H(X;u,v)=H(X\setminus Z;u,v)+H(Z;u,v)

whenever
Z
is a closed subvariety of
X
(see e.g.
[
59
, Theorem 1.1]
). Since
\mathcal{W}\setminus\mathcal{W}^{\circ}
is a union of open strata of smaller dimensions, we deduce from the induction hypothesis and (
A.1
) that
H(\mathcal{W}_{b}\setminus\mathcal{W}^{\circ}_{b};u,v)
is constant in
b
. By assumption,
\mathcal{W}
is smooth over
\mathcal{B}
, hence
H(\mathcal{W}_{b};u,v)
is also constant in
b
. Thus another application of (
A.1
) implies that
H(\mathcal{W}^{\circ}_{b};u,v)
is constant in
b
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
A.1
.

First assume that
(X,\Delta)
is klt. By the negativity lemma, we know that for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
we have
a(E,X_{i},\Delta_{i})\leq a(E,X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})
for all divisors
E
over
X
, and the inequality is strict for some
E
. By Lemma
A.3
, we see that

E_{\mathrm{st}}(X_{i},\Delta_{i};u,v)>E_{\mathrm{st}}(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1};u,v)

when
u,v\gg 0
. It follows that

(A.2)

E_{\mathrm{st}}(X_{i},\Delta_{i};u,v)\neq E_{\mathrm{st}}(X_{j},\Delta_{j};u,v)

whenever
i\neq j
. If there is an
\mathbb{R}
-Gorenstein family of pairs
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{B}
that contains every
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
as a fiber, then after possibly stratifying
\mathcal{B}
, we may assume that there is a crepant log resolution
\pi\colon(\mathcal{Y},\mathcal{G})\to(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})
, a projective morphism
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}\to\mathcal{B}
with an open immersion
\mathcal{Y}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{Y}}
over
\mathcal{B}
, and an
\mathbb{R}
-divisor
\overline{\mathcal{G}}
on
\overline{\mathcal{Y}}
such that
\mathcal{G}=\overline{\mathcal{G}}|_{\mathcal{Y}}
, the sub-pair
(\overline{\mathcal{Y}},\overline{\mathcal{G}})
is log smooth, every stratum of
(\overline{\mathcal{Y}},\overline{\mathcal{G}})
is smooth over
\mathcal{B}
, and
\mathcal{Y}
is a union of some open strata of
(\overline{\mathcal{Y}},\overline{\mathcal{G}})
. By Lemma
A.4
, we see that the stringy
E
-function
E_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathcal{Y}_{b},\mathcal{G}_{b};u,v)
(
b\in\mathcal{B}
) is constant on each connected component of
\mathcal{B}
, hence there are only finitely many possible stringy
E
-functions
E_{\mathrm{st}}(X_{i},\Delta_{i};u,v)
. It follows that the MMP must terminate in finitely many steps, otherwise we get a contradiction to (
A.2
).

In general, let
W_{i}
be the reduced non-klt locus of
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
. We claim that for any
i\in\mathbb{N}
and any sufficiently small
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
(which may depend on
i
), we have

(A.3)

a(E,X_{i},\Delta_{i}-\mathcal{I}_{W_{i}}^{\varepsilon})\leq a(E,X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1}-\mathcal{I}_{W_{i+1}}^{\varepsilon})

for all divisors
E
over
X
and the inequality is strict for some
E
. It is enough to prove this for prime divisors on some common log resolution
Y
of
(X_{i},\Delta_{i}+\mathcal{I}_{W_{i}})
and
(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1}+\mathcal{I}_{W_{i+1}})
. Let
E
be one such divisor. If the center of
E
on
X_{i}
is contained in the exceptional locus of the birational map
X_{i}\dashrightarrow X_{i+1}
(there is at least one such divisor), then

a(E,X_{i},\Delta_{i})<a(E,X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})

by the negativity lemma, hence the strict inequality in (
A.3
) holds for sufficiently small
\varepsilon
. As there are only finitely many such divisors
E
on
Y
, we can make a uniform choice of
\varepsilon
. If the center
\xi=c_{X_{i}}(E)
of
E
on
X_{i}
is not contained in the exceptional locus, then the map
X_{i}\dashrightarrow X_{i+1}
is an isomorphism at
\xi
and identifies the non-klt locus
W_{i}
with
W_{i+1}
around
\xi
. It follows that
a(E,X_{i},\Delta_{i})=a(E,X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1})
and
\mathrm{ord}_{E}(\mathcal{I}_{W_{i}})=\mathrm{ord}_{E}(\mathcal{I}_{W_{i+1}})
, hence (
A.3
) also holds. This proves the claim.

Note that the non-klt locus
W_{i}
is determined by the pair
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
. If there exists an
\mathbb{R}
-Gorenstein family
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})\to\mathcal{B}
that contains every pair
(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
as a fiber, then possibly after stratifying
\mathcal{B}
, we may assume that the non-klt locus
\mathcal{W}
of
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{D})
restricts to
W_{i}
on
X_{i}
. By passing to log resolutions and compactifications over
\mathcal{B}
as in the klt case and using Lemma
A.4
, we deduce that there are only finitely many possibilities for the functions
E_{\mathrm{st}}(X_{i},\Delta_{i}-\mathcal{I}_{W_{i}}^{\varepsilon};u,v)
(in the variables
\varepsilon,u,v
). On the other hand, by (
A.3
) and Lemma
A.3
we also have

E_{\mathrm{st}}(X_{i},\Delta_{i}-\mathcal{I}_{W_{i}}^{\varepsilon};u,v)>E_{\mathrm{st}}(X_{i+1},\Delta_{i+1}-\mathcal{I}_{W_{i+1}}^{\varepsilon};u,v)

when
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
and
u,v\gg 0
, hence

E_{\mathrm{st}}(X_{i},\Delta_{i}-\mathcal{I}_{W_{i}}^{\varepsilon};u,v)\neq E_{\mathrm{st}}(X_{j},\Delta_{j}-\mathcal{I}_{j}^{\varepsilon};u,v)

whenever
i\neq j
. Thus the MMP terminates in finitely many steps by the same argument as in the klt case.
∎

Proof of Corollary
A.2
.

If
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\cong(X,\Delta)
, then by repeating the same sequence of
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP steps, we get an infinite sequence of
(K_{X}+\Delta)
-MMP that only involves finitely many isomorphism classes of pairs. This contradicts Theorem
A.1
.
∎
